FREDERICK L. SORSABAL
Grand Master
October 12, 2005 – October 11, 2006

ii

FREDERICK L. SORSABAL
Grand Master – 2005-2006
Fred lives in Placerville, CA with his wife of 47 years, Marlene. They
have two sons and six grandchildren. He was born in Southern California
where they resided until his retirement in 1995.
Fred received his Bachelor of Arts Degree from California State
University, Long Beach in Political Science with an emphasis in Public
Administration. He started his career in Public Management in the City of
Anaheim. In 1970, Fred was appointed City Manager of the City of Costa
Mesa, Ca. where he served for 15 years. He also served the cities of El Segundo
and Mission Viejo before retirement.
His civic involvement has been numerous serving on the boards of
United Way; the Heart Association; and the foundations for hospitals in Mission
Viejo and Placerville. He has received the Rotary Paul Harris Fellow Award.
In October, 1963, he was raised in Azure Lodge #533 in Fullerton, Cal
and served as Master in 1990. He now is a member of Placerville Lodge #26.
His other Masonic involvement includes serving as President of the Placerville
Masonic Center Association; a Past Master Councilor and recipient of the
Active DeMolay Legion of Honor. He is a KCCH in Sacramento Scottish Rite
bodies, a member of the Long Beach Bodies of York Rite; a Past Potentate of El
Bekal Shrine and Associate Member of Ben Ali Shrine; Red Cross of
Constantine; the York Rite Sovereign College; Royal Order of Scotland;
Southern California Research Lodge; National Sojourners; and the Order of
Eastern Star. Fred served as President of the Board of Governors – Los Angeles
Shrine Hospital for Children and also the Scottish Rite Language Disorder
Center in Long Beach and is presently the Chairman of the Grand Master's
Conference of North America.
Brother Sorsabal has served on several Grand Lodge Committees and
was Grand Orator under Most Worshipful R. Stephen Doan. He served as a
Trustee for the Masonic Homes of California and was President of the Homes
Board. He was elected the Grand Master of Masons in California in October of
2005.

PUBLIC OPENING CEREMONY
Sunday, October 8, 2006
1:00 PM
The Public Opening of Grand Lodge was held in the Masonic
Auditorium of the California Masonic Memorial Temple, 1111 California
Street, San Francisco, California beginning at 1:00 p.m. The Grand Lodge was
raised by Worshipful Garry Erck, Master of Placerville Lodge No. 26.
Grand Master Frederick L. Sorsabal, accompanied by his Grand Lodge
Officers, entered the Auditorium to the sound of the organ, and assumed their
stations. The Grand Chaplain gave the invocation.
Great Creator of us all. The excitement and joy of this day
surrounds us all. We are like children anxiously waiting to open our
presents. Laughter so easily comes to our lips. How long we have
prepared for this hour! Help us to remember that this is not our doing
that bring us here, but only your grace and love. Your love surrounded
us at our birth even when we did not see it. Your love has been present
in that great book of nature and revelation, and in the countless acts of
others. For this we give you thanks.
In what we are about to say and do today, teach us to walk gently
in the path set before us. Temper our actions, give us the fortitude to
resist our own will, instead of following yours; may we be prudent in
choosing our words and casting our ballots; and let justice inform all
our actions.
We ask your special blessing on our brothers, and those serving
our country in the military withersoever dispersed. Keep them safe and
secure until they return home.
Enliven our imaginations that we can see through present problems
to future newness. Embolden us with Brotherly Love, Relief and
Truth, that we may live now as if the new had already come. In joy we
offer you our praise. Amen.
The Grand Master welcomed all in attendance to the Public Opening of
the 157th Annual Communication of the Grand Lodge of California. He thanked
the Master of Placerville Lodge for raising the Grand Lodge.
The Grand Marshal was directed to introduce the visiting dignitaries
from other Masonic jurisdictions and the leaders of the concordant and
appendant bodies of the Family of Freemasonry in California. The following
were present:

At the conclusion of the introductions, the Grand Master remarked, “It
is a special pleasure and a privilege to welcome you to our Annual
Communication. To each of you I express the appreciation of all Masons in
California.”
The Grand Master then directed the Grand Marshal to retire and with
the assistance of the Grand Commandery Knights Templar of the State of
California, present the Flag of Our Country before the Altar of Freemasonry.
This was followed by the singing of the National Anthem by Worshipful Vince
Parell, Master of Tehama Lodge No. 3.
The Grand Master then called on Norma Stilwell, Worthy Grand
Matron and Michael Cornell, Worthy Grand Patron of the Grand Chapter of
California, Order of the Eastern Star to bring greetings on behalf of the Family
of Freemasonry and our visitors from other jurisdictions.

Leadership Development Program Recognition
With Leadership Development as one of the highest priorities, the
Grand Lodge sponsored three training programs this year including two
Inspectors Seminars, four Wardens Retreats, and five Lodge Management
Certification programs. In all, nearly 1,000 participants received leadership
training this year.
The Lodge Management Certification Program consists of five courses
that are conducted simultaneously at a particular location. The courses include:
Hall Association Management, Lodge Finance, Membership Development,
Program Planning, and Lodge Administration. Each course is a full day in
length. At the end of each course, participants must pass an examination before
they are given a certification in that course. In order to complete more than one
course, a participant must attend more than one location or take the courses over
a number of years. This was the third year the program was offered. Eleven
members have already completed all five courses.
The Grand Master was joined at the podium by the following: Angel
Alvarez-Pagan, Woodbridge Lodge No. 131; Raymond Broomfield, Channel
Island Lodge No. 214; Gilbert Buck, Golden Trowel Norwalk Lodge No. 273;
David Chernik, Southern California Lodge No. 529; David Chesebro, King
David’s Lodge No. 209; David Hall, Fellowship Lodge No. 668; James

Matthews, Fresno Lodge No. 247; Scott Mitchell, Santa Cruz San Lorenzo
Valley Lodge No.38; and Neal Snyder, Paso Robles Lodge No. 286.
The Grand Master congratulated the members for completing the
Lodge Management Certification Program and presented each one with a
plaque.

Lillian Birchenall
The Grand Master then recognized Ms. Lillian Birchenall, a resident of
the Masonic Home, for challenging the lodges in California to raise at least
$100,000 to benefit Leadership Development. If that goal was met, Ms.
Birchenall would join in the cause in a very significant way. With this
motivation and the inspiration from her devotion to the fraternity, that goal was
met in a way that had never been accomplished before.
The Grand Master then presented Ms. Birchenall with a small token of
appreciation on behalf of the Masons of California.

Remarks from the Youth Orders
The Grand Master called on the four youth leaders of the three
Masonic youth orders in California throughout the day. Miss Shoghig
Manoukin, Grand Bethel Honored Queen, represented the International Order of
Job's Daughters. Following her remarks the Grand Master presented her with a
donation on behalf of the California Masonic Foundation to benefit the Job’s
Daughters leadership training program.
The Grand Master then called on Mr. Anthony Fernandez, Jr., Master
Councilor, Southern California Jurisdiction, Order of DeMolay for his remarks,
after which he presented a donation on behalf of the California Masonic
Foundation to benefit the DeMolay leadership training program.

Daniel Carter Beard Masonic Scouter Award
The Grand Marshal was directed to conduct the Daniel Carter Beard
Masonic Souter Award recipients West of the Altar. The Grand Master, with
the assistance of the Deputy Grand Master, presented the following brethren
with this award recognizing their service to youth through Scouting: John E.
Roberts, a member of Riviera Lodge No. 780; Jerry G. Plotke, a member of
Southern California Lodge No. 529; and Jerry G. Laiblin, a member of
Greenleaf Gardens Lodge No. 670.

Remarks from the Youth Orders
The Grand Master then called on Mr. John Nygard, Master Councilor,
Northern California Jurisdiction, Order of DeMolay for his remarks, after which
he presented a donation on behalf of the California Masonic Foundation to

Grand Master's Youth Support Award
The Grand Master announced that four brothers had been selected to
receive the Grand Master's Youth Support Award to recognize their outstanding
contribution to the Masonic youth orders. The Grand Master then called upon
Worshipful V. Kent Armstrong, Chairman of the Grand Lodge Youth Orders
Committee to assist him in presenting the Grand Master’s Youth Support Award
to the following honorees: Peter G. Gibson, a member of Oroville Lodge No.
103; Cline C. “Cub” Jack, a member of Las Palmas-Ponderosa Lodge No. 366;
Stewart King, a member of Eureka Lodge No. 16; and John A. Williams, a
member of Monterey Lodeg No.217.
The Grand Master directed the Grand Marshal to escort the recipients
to a place West of the Altar. The Grand Master and the Deputy Grand Master
then presented the awards while Brother Armstrong read the biography of each
honoree.
The distinguished Masons were saluted with the Public Grand Honors.
The Grand Master then asked the Grand Marshal to conduct the recipients to
seats in the auditorium.

Presentation of the Public Safety Service Program
Our Grand Master spent his entire professional career in public
administration. He started as the Assistant City Manager of Anaheim and
retired as the City Manager for El Segundo and Mission Viejo. During that time
he worked very closely with the police and fire departments of those cities,
where he witnessed, first hand, the heroic, life-threatening and life-saving work
of the men and women who serve our communities in those capacities.
Today, the service of our police officers and firefighters has grown
even more dangerous and their efforts even more heroic.

10

PROCEEDINGS OF THE

At this time, the Grand Master invited Michael Brown, Commissioner
of the California Highway Patrol, to the podium for his remarks, after which the
Grand Master introduced Richard Picciotto, the highest-ranking firefighter to
survive the World Trade Center collapse.
Chief Picciotto shared with those in attendance his experience on
September 11, 2001 and his tribute to the 343 firefighters and 2,400 civilians
who lost their lives that day.
To memorialize the respect and admiration our Grand Master has for
our police and firefighters, he commissioned a medal to be struck – the Grand
Master’s Medal for Distinguished Service in Public Safety. Both Commissioner
Brown and Chief Picciotto assisted the Grand Master in the presentation of
these service medals to 60 brethren who risk their lives each day for our
protection and safety.

DEVOTIONAL SERVICE
The Grand Master then observed that as the program drew to a close,
we would turn our thoughts inward and to the attention of the Grand Chaplain
for an Inspirational Message.
The Grand Master then called upon the Grand Chaplain, the Very
Reverend Mark R. Sandstrom, for his message. The complete text of the Grand
Chaplain’s Message can be found in Appendix G.
The Grand Master thanked the Very Reverend Mark Sandstrom for his
truly inspirational message.
The Grand Master concluded by thanking all for attending the Public
Opening of the 157th Annual Communication of Grand Lodge and reminded
everyone that Richard Picciotto would be in the lobby signing copies of his
book, “Last Man Down”. The Grand Master led the Grand Lodge Officers out
of the auditorium.

ANNUAL COMMUNICATION
MONDAY MORNING, OCTOBER 9, 2006
The 157th Annual Communication of the Grand Lodge of Free and
Accepted Masons of the State of California was called to order by Grand Master
Frederick L. Sorsabal, on Monday, October 9, 2006.
Worshipful Rodney Laderas, Master of Riviera Lodge No. 780, raised
the opening session of Grand Lodge.

Reception of the Past Grand Masters
The Grand Master welcomed the brethren and directed the Grand
Marshal to present the Past Grand Masters of the Grand Lodge of California at
the Altar. The Grand Marshal then introduced each Past Grand Master,
following which the Grand Master, accompanied by the Deputy Grand Master,
personally greeted each one. The Grand Master asked the brethren to join him
in saluting our Past Grand Masters by the battery of three times three. The Past
Grand Masters were then escorted to seats reserved for them in the auditorium.
The Grand Master then directed the Grand Secretary to send to each absent Past
Grand Master the usual letter of greeting from the Grand Lodge.

Roll Call
The Grand Secretary was asked to call the roll of Grand Lodge
Officers, Past Grand Masters, and Past Grand Officers, and to ascertain if a
constitutional number was present to convene the Grand Lodge of California.
The following were present as indicated by their response to the calling of the
roll:

The Grand Secretary announced that the Committee on Credentials had
reported that at the close of Registration on Sunday, there were 1,068 Master
Masons registered as of the end of the day, with at least twenty percent of the
Lodges chartered by this Grand Lodge represented, and that therefore a quorum
was present as required for the transaction of business.
The Grand Secretary reported that all Lodges have paid their annual
per capita assessment to Grand Lodge except for the following Lodges:
1.

Tuscan Maxwell No. 182
Grand Lodge Opening

The Grand Master then opened Grand Lodge in ample form. The
Grand Marshal and the Deacons retired to escort the Flag of the United States of
America into the Auditorium, which was followed by the Pledge of Allegiance.

Introductions
The following were asked to rise as groups for recognition: Hiram
Award Recipients, Diamond Veteran Award Recipients, Golden Veteran Award
Recipients, Worshipful Masters, Lodge Secretaries, Grand Lodge Committee
members, members of Constitutional Boards, District Inspectors, and Visiting
Brethren from other jurisdictions. Each group was greeted with a round of
applause.

Memorial Service
The Grand Master called on the Very Reverend Mark R. Sandstrom,
Grand Chaplain, to present a service of remembrance for our departed brethren.

Woody Allen said, “I'm not afraid to die; I just don't want to be there
when it happens.”
When we are young death is not a reality. We see our favorite cartoon
characters squashed by an anvil, but they are up in the next frame. We see our
heroes shot in one movie, but they are alive in the next.
But at some point, something or someone close to us dies and death
starts to become a reality. First a grandparent, or an aunt or uncle. And then a
spouse or sibling. Death moves from the abstract to something quite existential.
James the Apostle, said: “For what is your life? It is even a vapor that
appears for a little while and then vanishes away.”

GRAND LODGE F.& A.M. OF CALIFORNIA

15

James was reminding us that life is a fragile thing. The death
messenger comes to every person who draws a breath of life in this world. NO
one can escape it . . . Death comes to the King in her palace . . . Death comes to
the beggar beside the road . . . Death comes to those who don't want to live . . .
and Death comes to those who want desperately want to live.
Death is a reality and it comes to each of us.
We know life is a gift, a gift that we have a share for a little while, and
we vaguely realize that death is a part of living. We are never prepared for the
reality of death. Its tragic swiftness sometimes leaves us shocked. The hurt is
deep and real.
But life is a gift! It is only when we see it as a gift that we can conquer
the mountain of grief that comes with the loss – the death of someone we love.
Even in the midst of the pain and loss, we can look to God with a sense of
gratitude, with a sense of thanksgiving.
I do not mean to say that such a perspective makes things easier. It
does not lessen the pain. It does not dispel our grief. But, I believe that it does
make the pain bearable when we remember that life is a gift.
And so in these moments let us recall the memories of our departed
Brothers. Those memories are your own particular gift that God has given you.
No one of us has the exact same memory, for they are all different because of
time place and experience. We all have our own unique, personal gift of our
Brothers.
In these kind of moments, it's easy to let sorrow take over, but I hope
that you will hold onto the special memories. When you do, you will realize life
is a gift you shared for a little while. We are not totally separated from our
Brothers, for we each carry them with us. We are stamped by their influence.
Their image is on us. They live in us in the values we shared and the memories
we made together. Like a piano concerto by Chopin lives on after the composer
dies, everything that endeared them to us, lives on in us, for they have shaped
who we are. And so, in the days ahead, the gulf of this separation will not seem
so great.
Cecil B. DeMille, the renowned movie director of world fame was also
a man of deep faith. It was he who first dramatically depicted the Biblical
stories on the screen. Shortly before his death, he wrote the following:
“One day as I was lying in a canoe, a big black beetle came out of the
water and climbed into the canoe. I watched it idly for some time. Under the
heat of the sun, the beetle proceeded to die. Then a strange thing happened. Her
glistening black shell cracked all the way down her back. Out a came a
shapeless mass, quickly transformed into a beautiful, brilliantly colored life. As
I watched in fascination, there gradually unfolded iridescent wings from which
the sunlight flashed a thousand colors. The wings spread wide, as if in worship

16

PROCEEDINGS OF THE

to the sun. The blue-green body took shape. Before my eyes had occurred a
metamorphosis – the transformation of a hideous beetle into a gorgeous
dragonfly, which started dipping and soaring over the water. But the body it
had left behind still clung to my canoe. I had witnessed what seemed to be a
miracle. Out of the mud had come a beautiful new life.”
“And the thought came to me,” continued Mr. DeMille, “that if the
Creator works such wonders with the lowliest of creatures, what may not be in
store for the human spirit?”
Death is the shedding of those parts which are no longer of value in the
new life being entered. Yet putting them aside in no way disturbs, threatens,
nor destroys the future expression of our total being. We are but set free from
the old, and will be given a new body to glorify God.
Our funeral ritual and our third degree affirms this with the symbol of
the acacia. By it we are reminded that we have an imperishable part within us,
which shall survive all earthly existence, and which will never, never die.
My brothers, with hearts and minds mingled with grief and joy, with
solace and regret, we recall the memory of our departed brethren.
Let us not forget that while we recall the memories of Brothers loved
and known to us, there are also anonymous Brethren who have passed away
without anyone around them and without anyone knowing it or memorializing
it.
In the small hamlet of Jordan Station, Ontario, Canada, at the
intersection of Red Maple and Bridgeport Drive, there is a small cemetery
named Oak Lawn Cemetery. Located at the very back of this cemetery
overlooking the banks of the historic Twenty Mile Creek rests the remains of a
Mason. The inscription on his most unusual gravestone perhaps best tells the
story of his internment in this pioneer cemetery.

HERE
Lieth the Remains
OF
AN UNKNOWN BROTHER
Whose body was washed ashore
Near the residence of
ABRAM MARTIN ESQ LOUTH
On 20th April 1877
This tombstone is erected to show that
while deceased had only on his person
certain symbols to distinguish him as
a Freemason yet were they sufficient
to secure for the remains fraternal

GRAND LODGE F.& A.M. OF CALIFORNIA

17

sympathy and Christian sepulture
Dead Voiceless Battered Tempest tossed
A stranger Friendless and unknown
The wave gave up its dead. A Brother
came and saw. And raised above
his lonely head This sculptured stone
The mystic points of Fellowship prevail
Death’s Gavel cannot break that sacred tie
Gainst light the Powers of Night can
naught prevail. To live in hearts we leave
behind is not to Die
Memorial Service Prayer
Because it is true that “In My Father's House There are Many
Mansions,” make sure we don't get cramped today in too small a space. Keep
the doors of our minds and our work open that they can breathe and sense the
breeze of your promises. Your promise is everlasting life. Your promise is
eternal hope. Your promise is constant comfort. We know we can rely on you.
We give thanks, not as we ought, but as we are able, for all that is good
and all that is permanent. Our memories will carry us through the hard times
because they are good. Our hope will be in you and in remembering how you
got us through all kinds of trouble, both in life and in death. For every good
thing, we praise your name. For all that we missed, we absolve ourselves in
you. For kind words left unspoken, forgive us. And open the doors of your
great mansion, both here and beyond. Amen.

Most Worshipful R. Stephen Doan delivered a eulogy for the late Past
Grand Master, Most Worshipful Robert A. Crigler.

18

PROCEEDINGS OF THE

EULOGY
ROBERT A. CRIGLER
Robert Arthur Crigler, the 103rd Grand Master of Masons in California,
was born in Denver, Colorado, on June 28, 1916, the son of Robert Oscar
Crigler and Mary Frances Powers. Bob’s fourth great-grandfather, Jacob
Crigler, was born in Germany and came to America in 1717 to escape economic
devastation in Europe and to enjoy greater religious freedom in America. He
settled in Germanna in western Virginia, then part of the perimeter protecting
the colony. As these things sometimes happen, some of my brother’s and my
ancestors were on the same boat to Virginia with Jacob Crigler, and they also
settled in Germanna. Bob and my brother’s and my paternal grandmother were
fourth cousins.
Bob’s father was an automobile mechanic. With the economic decline
in the late 1920’s, the family moved to Chicago whence Bob’s mother came.
Sometime after 1930, the Criglers (Bob’s parents, Bob, and his sisters Barbara
and Billie) moved to the Los Angeles area where Bob attended Huntington Park
High School, graduating in the class of 1933, Los Angeles City College and
U.C.L.A. Bob was also a graduate of the Los Angeles Stock Exchange Institute.
Bob started his business career as a stock and bond trader in 1938. The
war in 1942 interrupted, and through 1943 he served as a Technical
Representative for the United States Air Force in the African theatre. Returning
to civilian life, Bob spent two years with Hydraulic Engineering Company as
General Manager before establishing his own business in insurance and
industrial land development. His keen business sense and awareness of value,
as well as a talent for organization, brought an increasing flow of clients, both
local and Eastern, who relied on his judgment in the purchase of land and the
development of buildings in the then expanding West Coast. In later years, Bob
was an insurance broker with his own company.
On August 5, 1939, Bob married Dena Davis. Son Bob, Jr. was born
the next year and daughter Karen in 1945. Bob and Dena were longtime
members of Plymouth Congregational Church where Bob served as Lay Leader,
Moderator and President of the Board of Trustees.
Bob’s father was made a Mason at Angeles Mesa Lodge No. 625 in
1942. In November of 1944, Bob joined John Marshall Lodge No. 636 (now
through consolidation part of Ionic-Composite Lodge No. 520), becoming
Worshipful Master in 1953. He thereafter served as Officers Coach from 1954.
He was appointed a member of the Columbia Temple Committee in 1960 and
as Inspector of District No. 522 in 1961. In 1963, Bob was appointed to fill the
vacancy created by the sudden death of Assistant Grand Lecturer Ralph W.
Collins. The strong ties of respect and brotherly love forged during his years as

GRAND LODGE F.& A.M. OF CALIFORNIA

19

Assistant Grand Lecturer were evidenced in the then Fifth Division and beyond
when Bob was elected Junior Grand Warden in 1966. Bob is among only a
handful of men who have been elected Junior Grand Warden without being the
recommendee. Bob skipped the office of Senior Grand Warden when the entire
line advanced on the resignation of Deputy Grand Master John Porter in 1967.
Bob was installed Grand Master on October 18, 1968.
Among Bob’s other Masonic affiliations, he was a member of Signet
Chapter No. 57, Royal Arch Masons, and Los Angeles Commandery No. 9,
Knights Templar. A member of and Past Wise Master of the Los Angeles
Scottish Rite Bodies, he was coroneted an Inspector General Honorary of the
Thirty-third Degree in 1991. He was a member and Past Sovereign of St.
Gabriel Conclave, Red Cross of Constantine, and holder of the Honorary
DeMolay Legion of Honor. He served the Grand Lodge of India as its Official
Representative near the Grand Lodge of California.
Civically, Bob was recognized with the John F. Kennedy Award for
outstanding and long-term service as Chairman of the Beverly Hills Draft Board
and by the Gold Salute Award of the Los Angeles Junior Chamber of
Commerce.
One of the most significant events of Bob’s year as Grand Master was
an All Masonic Family Reception at the Los Angeles Sports Arena with over
13,000 people in attendance.
Bob demanded of himself and others the standards of conduct and
selfless devotion to the duties of a Master Mason learned at the altar of
Masonry, both noble aims. However, Bob’s year as Grand Master was not
without some disharmony. We periodically see in our lodges conflict between
the Master and Lodge Secretary. The same can happen on occasion in our
Grand Lodge. Bob was sometimes at odds with others in Grand Lodge
leadership. As a result, we did not see much of Bob at Masonic activities after
his year as Grand Master. Bob had tremendous leadership and personal skills
which were regrettably lost to us when he became a Past Grand Master.
Bob’s wife Dena died in 1983. Bob then married Claudine with whom
he spent the balance of his life. Bob enjoyed those years as he and Claudine
traveled the world. Bob’s famous sense of humor and Claudine’s support
sustained him as he dealt with the progression of Parkinson’s disease, to which
he succumbed on August 8, 2006, six weeks following his ninetieth birthday.
We extend our condolences to Bob’s family and many sorrowing
friends.

Master, as the Supervisor of Elections. He requested Most Worshipful Brother
Whitaker to meet the tellers in Meeting Room Number 104 behind the stage and
supervise the counting of the ballots, and when counted, report the results to the
Grand Lodge.
After determining that all had cast ballots, the Grand Master declared
the ballot closed. The Grand Master called the Grand Lodge free from restraint
until the sound of the gavel in the East.
After explaining the procedure used to count the ballots, Most
Worshipful C. Ray Whitaker reported that Worshipful Brother Paul D. Hennig
had received a majority of the votes cast. Upon Worshipful Brother Hennig’s
acceptance, the Grand Master declared him elected.

Grand Lodge Called from Labor to Refreshment
The Grand Master then called Grand Lodge from labor to refreshment
for the purpose of installing the Grand Lodge Officers for the ensuing Grand
Lodge Year. The Installation was announced as being at 11:00 a.m. on
Wednesday, October 11, 2006.

GRAND MASTER’S MESSAGE
It is my pleasure to welcome the delegates and members of the Grand
Lodge of California Free and Accepted Masons to this the 157th Annual
Communication of our Grand Lodge. It is my hope that this session, assembled
in peace and harmony, will continue throughout our time in that same spirit.
I also want to extend to our distinguished guests from other
jurisdictions a most hearty welcome to our Annual Communication. Our Grand
Lodge is truly honored that you would be able to find the time out of your busy
schedules to be with us and we hope that you will enjoy your visit to this
beautiful City of San Francisco. If at any time, we can assist you please to make
your visit more enjoyable, please ask.
Section 1060 of the California Masonic Code requires the Grand
Master to "present at each Annual Communication, a written message outlining
the following: (1) All of his official acts during the year; (2) the general
conditions of Masonry within the Jurisdiction; and (3) Recommending such
legislation as he deems necessary or expedient for the welfare of the Craft".
Today, I stand before you to explain my stewardship of the Craft of the
Most Worshipful Grand Lodge of Free and Accepted Masons of California for
the Masonic Year 2005-2006. The Theme for this Masonic Year was "UNITED
WE STAND, TOGETHER WE CAN MAKE A DIFFERENCE. THE
FAMILY OF FREEMASONRY". We can succeed in our endeavors only if the
Family of Freemasonry comes together and stands as one.
It doesn't seem possible that a year has passed since you granted me the
great privilege to serve as the 141st Grand Master. My Brothers you have
honored me with this privilege and I hope that I have not disappointed you.
Before I begin my discussion of the state of the Craft, I would like to
thank all of you for allowing me to serve as Grand Master. The path before me
was well laid out and was relatively easy to follow. Those outstanding Masons
who served in this capacity before me, assisted in creating a wonderful
environment for each successor. But, my Brothers, you have helped immensely
by your good counsel and awesome enthusiasm.
Our Grand Lodge Officers this year were outstanding. Most were able
to attend the events up and down this Jurisdiction. Their dedication and
commitment to Masonry is greatly appreciated by your Grand Master. Their
enthusiasm continued even as the year was coming to a close. Special thanks go
to their wives who were especially helpful at each event. My special thanks to
our Grand Marshal, Wor Vince Carter and his wife Gail. The position of Grand
Marshal is probably as hectic as that of Grand Master. When you share the car
for hours driving you really get to know people. My thanks to you Wor. Vince.
I also want to give accolades to two retiring Grand Lodge Officers. The Most
Worshipful William Holsinger, Grand Treasurer, and Very Worshipful Hal

38

PROCEEDINGS OF THE

Macy, Grand Lecturer have both decided to retire. Their work for our Craft has
been outstanding and I know that they both will be missed but certainly not
forgotten. Saying thank you is not enough for the untiring work and advice you
have given this Grand Lodge over the years. Please stand and let's give them
the Public Grand Honor of Masonry by the Battery of Three Times Three.
Together, Brethren!
My thanks also go out to our wonderful Grand Lodge Staff. This year
was certainly made easier with their true professionalism and counsel. Very
Worshipful John Cooper, Grand Secretary, what can I say? Thanks are not
enough. Worshipful Allan Casalou, Assistant Grand Secretary for taking on the
added responsibilities this year and working those long hours to make Grand
Lodge run smoothly. Thanks to Brother Doug Ismail for coordinating the
campaign for our Grand Master's Project this year. WOW! Also to your staff
for running a great campaign for our Philanthropies. Those who will receive
service from this campaign will be greatly indebted to our Craft. Sister Kim
Hegg, for training the Secretaries in our iMember program and for running the
Warden's Retreats. What a Job! To the rest of the Staff - you make me so
proud, always ready to help.
My thanks also go to my family for being so supportive - our sons - we
have not seen too much this year - thanks for your patience and understanding.
Lastly to my wife Marlene! My grateful appreciation for your love and
commitment to our Fraternity. As most of us realize, we couldn't carry out our
responsibilities without the support of our spouse. Sometimes you laugh,
sometimes you cry, but having the knowledge of this support gives one the
desire to carry on. Thank you my love from the bottom of my heart for your
untiring efforts this year.
Now to the State of our Craft:
I would like to ask each of you to reconsider your commitment to our
Masonic Philanthropies. We have notice a downward trend in our member
participation even though the contribution from those who do participate has
significantly increased. We know that many of our Appendent and Concordant
bodies have philanthropies also and I am sure many of you participate with
those programs. However, we must never forget our Masonic Homes and the
California Masonic Foundation when we are thinking about our charitable
dollars. The California Masonic Home is providing care and shelter for more
qualified residents now than in several years and the costs to provide that care is
ever increasing. One outstanding program within the Masonic Homes is the
Masonic Outreach Service which provides care for qualified applicants in their
own home or facility near there home if we are not able to provide shelter at
either of our 2 homes. The Bush Foundation of Los Angeles has again provided
$100,000 for the care of Masons and widows from the Los Angeles area
through Masonic Outreach Service. We are grateful for that participation and I
would like to thank the Board of Directors of the Bush Foundation and MW

GRAND LODGE F.& A.M. OF CALIFORNIA

39

Melville Nahin for this wonderful leadership grant. The California Masonic
Foundation continues to support the educational programs of this Grand Lodge
and also need consideration of your charitable dollar.
While we have witnessed an increase in the number of applications to
our lodges throughout this Jurisdiction, our membership numbers continue to
decrease primarily to deaths. Our lodges must make a concerted effort to see
that the Lodge remains relevant to their membership and develop programs that
will ensure that the members and their families remain active in the Lodge. The
leadership of each lodge must see that those members who are withdrawing
and/or being suspended because of Non-Payment of Dues are personally
contacted to see the reasons for this action. We must admit to ourselves that
Masonry is not for every person who petitions, however the leaders of the
Lodge must determine the reasons for withdrawal. Suspension for NonPayment of Dues is another significant problem in this jurisdiction and again the
leadership of the lodges needs to make a bigger effort to ensure that these
Brothers do not have a significant financial problem that needs to be addressed.
It is a shame to see the number of long-time members being suspended without
having been personally contacted.
A great concern is that too many of our applicants are not advancing
through their degrees and again lodge leadership must take corrective action to
make sure that these men continue on their path to becoming Master Masons.
That action must be taken now! It is quite obvious to me that our lodges have
either not been providing or have not taken the time to see that members of the
lodge who are appointed to the Investigating Committee are properly prepared
to go to an applicants home and discuss Masonry with the applicant and his
family. Ours in an organization that makes good men better and we must ensure
that when our members recommend a man to make an application for Masonry
that they really know this person and not just to sign an application so that the
Lodge will get members. We are still too concerned about numbers and we
react to that instead of really knowing and understanding the needs of the
applicant. Remember that QUALITY ATTRACTS QUALITY!
In some of our lodges, the leadership has not taken their obligations
and commitments seriously enough. Grand Lodge provides the necessary
programs to assist the Lodge leadership to improve their skills to run their
Lodge, however we still see less than 50% of our lodges attending any of these
workshops. Being a Lodge Leader is not something you are born with; it is a
skill that must be continually honed and improved. Our lodges must see how
beneficial it is to have qualified men in the leadership seats in their lodge. In this
matter, I was able to provide four (4) scholarships to Junior Wardens to attend
the leadership sessions. We are observing that our Lodges are being led and
supported by a limited number of members. It is my belief that this will lead to
more and more consolidations of our Lodges. I believe that this is not to the
betterment of Craft Masonry in California.
When two weak Lodges

40

PROCEEDINGS OF THE

consolidate, it now leaves us with one weak Lodge that will still not be active in
the community unless men step forward to take the mantel of leadership and
provide the programs necessary to make Masonry again relevant in that
community. In my opinion, this is what the Grand Lodge Leadership Program
provides and our lodges must see that their leaders participate in the Warden's
Retreats and the Lodge Certification Programs. I am pleased to announce that at
the end of October, our first E-Learning session will be available. This will deal
with Lodge Finances. Please take advantage of this program. These programs
provide the tools for success!
I am pleased to inform you that this year we were able to grant Charters
to 2 lodges and issue a Dispensation for a 3rd. Ararat Lodge #848 and
Panamericana Lodge #849 were given their Charters early in this Grand Lodge
year and I was pleased to issue a Dispensation for a lodge to be formed in the
Masonic Homes at Union City called Siminoff Daylight Lodge UD. We will
watch with enthusiasm as these lodges continue forward.
I must sadly report that in some of our lodges we have seen an increase
of disharmony partly due to some of our members joining and being active in a
Clandestine Order called the Grand and Glorious Order Knights of the Creeping
Serpents or the Snakes. It apparently requires Masonic prerequisite to belong.
The last two years, we have attempted to identify what this organization is,
however the alleged members refuse to come forward and explain their actions.
This has led to much disharmony in several lodges and too much time spent by
Grand Lodge to see that the organization either attempt recognition or cease to
exist. Great concern has been expressed as to how this organization has
attempted to control some lodges in this jurisdiction. If the prerequisite is to be
a Mason to join this group, you must cease participation until the organization is
properly recognized or my Brothers, suffer the consequences of violating our
California Masonic Code. It is reported that some of their actions violate the
very principles of our gentle Craft. Good counsel has been whispered, but it is
evident that the counsel is being ignored. We can not afford to have happen in
California what happened several years ago in the Grand Jurisdiction of New
York. Negative publicity is not what is needed today. I would hope that if
those of you who still care to belong to an organization such as this, that you
consider leaving our Fraternity before you destroy it! Harmony is the strength
and beauty of all societies, especially of ours.
Reportable Actions
As indicated above, disharmony has existed in several of our lodges
and I have had to take certain action to attempt to rectify the problems. In some
cases, a word of advice to the Lodge from the Inspector or the Assistant Grand
Lecturer has been sufficient; however in several cases more drastic action was
needed. I will summarize those now:

GRAND LODGE F.& A.M. OF CALIFORNIA

41

Continuation of Suspensions from previous years:
South West Lodge #283 - Charter returned to the Lodge in August,
2006 as they had met all the requirements placed on them.
Abel Lodge #146 - allowed to consolidate with Willits Lodge #231
now Ridgewood Lodge #146 as of February 1, 2006.
Marin Lodge #191 suspension was continued however I returned their
Charter to them on May 10, 2006 as they had met all the requirements
placed on them and peace and harmony has returned to this Lodge.
New Suspensions:
Sotoyome-Curtis Lodge #123 suspended their Charter as of February 1,
2006 as they were unable to qualify officers; Charter was returned to
the Lodge on October 5, 2006 as they had met the requirements placed
on them to have their Officer qualified and an interim Master
appointed.
Chula Vista Lodge #626 - March 6, 2006 suspended. Charter was
returned to this Lodge in September, 2006.
Gardena Moneta Lodge #372; Suspended their Charter on June 19,
2006 and reinstated their Charter effective October 3, 2006 as they
completed all the requirements placed on them.
Tuscan Maxwell Lodge #182; suspended the Charter on April 10,
2006.
Torrance University Lodge #394; suspended the Charter on and
reinstated their Charter effective October 2, 2006.
In addition, I have found it necessary to remove from their positions as
Worshipful Masters in the following lodges:
Mountain View-DeAnza Lodge #194: Gardena Moneta Lodge #372:
and Torrance University Lodge #394. The Master of Gardena Moneta Lodge
#372 was reinstated at the same time the Charter was returned.
As administrative problems within our lodges begin to rise, I would
like to recommend to our incoming Grand Master, that an organizational study
be made this coming year to evaluate and recommend a more effective structure
to assist the Grand Master in handling these administrative problems. In my
opinion, the present organization utilizing the Inspectors to assist in handling the
problems has not worked in its entirety. The Inspectors readily admit that more
than 60% of their time involves administrative problems, but yet they are not
recommended for that expertise but basically for their Ritual acumen. A new
and more effective structure may be required to ameliorate this problem and
thus would free up the time of the Inspector to see that our Ritual in California

42

PROCEEDINGS OF THE

becomes uniform again. A report should be made by the Long Range Planning
Committee at the 2007 Annual Communication to determine whether a change
should be made or not.
As you recall, at the last Annual Communication we adopted a
Strategic Plan for Grand Lodge and gave your Grand Master and the Executive
Committee the ability to implement its Strategic Initiatives. This year I
emphasized the following:
MEMBERSHIP - Again this year, we have seen an influx of
applications coming to our lodges. What good news this is! Interest in
Masonry appears to be on the rise and each Lodge has an obligation to cultivate
that interest. Our "Pass It On" Program WORKS! And those lodges using the
program have shown an increase in applications. If your Lodge does not use it shame on you. This year we have 100 of our lodges who met the criteria of
raising 5 or more Master Masons. They will be honored later at this Annual
Communication. Our lodges are doing a better job in advancing candidates
through better mentoring and a desire to see their members become Master
Masons. We can do a far better job however!
Member retention still appears to be a problem. Our lodges must
initiate a more aggressive program to see why their members are withdrawing
and/or not paying their dues. We have also seen too may lodges not checking
on our elderly members whose dues are not paid and then just suspending them.
This trend must be reversed and personal contacts made with these brothers to
see what the problem is! Lodge Masters and Wardens; please review the
attitude in your Lodge concerning this problem.
Membership cultivation must include knowing the applicant. In the
past we have rushed to receive applicants without fully knowing him. We did
this due to our philosophy of having numbers in our lodges instead of quality WE ARE A QUALITY FRATERNITY, not a quantity Fraternity. Remember
QUALITY ATTRACTS QUALITY.
COMMUNITY INVOLVEMENT - What better way to have the
community understand who and what we are by involving our members in our
communities. Many of our lodges have participated with the Masonic Family in
the Make a Difference Month Program. Several lodges planned activities to
celebrate this national program. I want to congratulate each of you for getting
involved and as a reminder; this month continues to be Make a difference
Month. While it may too late for the 2006 year, you Senior Wardens should
start planning now with the Masonic Organizations which meet in your
building, what you can do this next year. You can make a difference in your
community.
MASONIC STUDENT ASSISTANCE PROGRAM - I want to single
out Naval Lodge #87, under the leadership of Wor. Robert Wilson, Master.

GRAND LODGE F.& A.M. OF CALIFORNIA

43

This Lodge decided that they would like to support more of their public school
teachers in a program that could be conducted in their area rather than sending
them to Covina. By utilizing their own facilities, this Lodge provided the
program for 120 attendees; 108 were teachers and administrators along with 12
observers. All of the participants were from schools within Solano County and
the program was provided through funding from this Lodge only. They plan
another Workshop this next year. My congratulations to Naval Lodge #87 for
stepping forward and taking on this project. It will provide their area with well
trained classroom teachers.
PUBLIC SCHOOLS - many of our lodges have a good program but
again I want to point out some who have come to my attention. Inyo Lodge
#221 provides bicycles for students who achieve not only in academics but
citizenship; Keith Lodge #187 provides dictionaries for 4th graders; Volcano
Lodge #56 provides bicycles for students reading books; Oakland Durant
Rockridge Lodge #188 for providing, since 1997, $220,000 in undergraduate
scholarships for deserving students. In fact one of their recipients is pursuing a
career in nursing and is serving an internship at Shriner's Hospital for Burns in
Galveston, TX. I want to congratulate Arcadia Lodge #278 for providing many
scholarships to students in the Arcadia School District upon graduation. They
had over 300 in attendance for the award luncheon program which not only
included the students, but most of the faculty and parents. Another outstanding
public schools program is from Heartland Lodge #576 called the "Super G
Program". This program for Kindergarten and grades 1 - 4 provides an avenue
to show off a student's achievement each month not for academic achievement
but more for doing things for the first time, like tying their shoes or dressing
without help, paying attention in class, completing assignments, learning to
draw by themselves. Activities are rewarded with a certificate and bumper
sticker. This program is well received by the school districts and parents. These
are but a few of our lodges who are doing a great job in assisting and protecting
our public schools and I know there are many more of you doing the same.
Keep up the good work.
MASONIC YOUTH - I have attempted to assist all our Masonic Youth
Organizations this year. It is very important to not only Craft Masonry's future,
but also to the Family of Freemasonry and society in general that our youth
groups prosper and grow. I have called them our Masonic Farm teams.
However, if we do not aggressively support them, not only financially, but with
our physical support, how will they know and see the good tenets our order
teaches? I want to thank those lodges who assisted our youth by providing
financial resources so that their programs can go forward. My Brothers, return
and assist your DeMolay Chapters, Job's Daughter's Bethels and Rainbow
Assemblies. They are waiting to see you and the future of the Family of
Freemasonry depends on them. I want to thank those lodges in Southern

44

PROCEEDINGS OF THE

California who support the Southern California DeMolay Jurisdiction for their
annual Conclave.
MASONIC EDUCATION - or as I am calling it MASONIC
FORMATION. The Grand Master's Project for 2006 included fund raising for
an important element of our Fraternity. It is an approach that will develop an
integrated program to facilitate a more comprehensive approach to Masonic
education, which at best in this jurisdiction, has been episodic, incomplete, and
even inaccurate. We all should realize that becoming a Mason does not end
when we are raised to the Sublime Degree of Master Mason, but instead is a
life-long learning experience that will not cease until the "sand in the hourglass" runs out. I believe we are witnessing a renaissance and have a window of
opportunity that must not be missed. It is my belief that men joining the
Fraternity today are seeking this type of education environment and it was with
attitude that our fund-raising was successful. I want to express my gratitude to
the following lodges that so promptly "step up to the plate" and granted the
California Masonic Foundation sizable donations for this program: Riviera
Lodge #780; South Bay Lodge #421; Vesper Lodge #84; California #1; Napa
Valley Lodge #93 who gave the first major gift to the campaign; Downey
United Lodge #220; Phoenix Lodge #144; Job's Daughter's International
through a contribution from the Supreme Bethel Session held in California in
2005; York Rite Bodies of Los Angeles; Orange County Lodges; Metropolitan
Lodge #352; Irvine Valley Lodge #671; Beach Cities Lodge #753; Bellflower
Lodge #320; Natoma Lodge #64 Vesper Lodge #84; Placerville Lodge #26; and
Anaheim #207. Several other lodges, along with several individual Brothers,
also participated in this fund raising endeavor.
My thanks also go to the Brothers who assisted in the call to assist.
They will be honored later in this Annual Communication. Very Worshipful
John L. Cooper, Grand Secretary, in his Report to the Craft will more
adequately discuss the program and where we will go from here, and also
demonstrate to us a portion of a DVD that will be available that will be available
to assist our lodges and candidate coaches a more visible way to instruct
candidates and eventually be used in the Lectures during our Degrees. My
Brothers, this Masonic Formation Program will only be successful if you and
the members of your Lodge participate. We all must once again understand the
importance of applying the teachings of Freemasonry to our daily lives. Only
then will our neighbors and our communities understand the importance of our
Masonic Principles to our society. As I have said all this year, you must be the
missionaries and practice out of our lodge rooms those great lessons we are
taught in it. They are NOT secret and must be shown to society but can only be
done so if we practice them. Studying Masonic symbolism, history and
organization can be interesting and exciting; the goal of Masonic Formation is
to translate the lessons and experiences one gain from Masonry into our daily

GRAND LODGE F.& A.M. OF CALIFORNIA

45

life. We must learn to apply the great lessons of Freemasonry and thus we will
make the Craft relevant to our society today.
My Brothers, I am extremely optimistic about Freemasonry in this
jurisdiction. We all know it is a premier fraternal organization that does so
much good. We must take every opportunity to talk about Freemasonry; every
opportunity to write and show off Freemasonry; and if the opportunity is not
there, then we should create that opportunity. Remember that what ever
Freemasonry is to be, it is because of you and me.
As in all organizations, there comes a time when one must step aside
and let the next person bring his personality into play. We are sad, when that
time comes, because the time was too short, but happy that we believe we have
accomplished the best our ability will allow us.
I want to leave you with this thought entitled "At Days End" by John
Hall. Is anyone happier now because you passed this way? Does anyone
remember that you spoke to him today? The day is almost over, as its toiling
time is through; is there anyone now to utter a kindly word of you? Can you say
tonight, in parting with the day that is slipping fast, that you helped a single
brother of the many you passed? Is a single person rejoicing over what you did
or said; does the friend whose hope is fading, now with renewed courage look
ahead? Did you waste the day, or lose it? Was it well or sorely spent? Did you
leave a trail of kindness, or a bleak scar of discontent? As you close your eyes
in slumber, do you think the Great Architect will say, "You have earned one
more tomorrow by the good works you did today?"
Thank you for giving me this wonderful honor.
Respectfully submitted,
Frederick L. Sorsabal, Grand Master

65

REPORT OF THE DEPUTY GRAND MASTER
2006-2007 Grand Lodge Budget
Melvyn B. Stein, Deputy Grand Master
As Deputy Grand Master it is my duty pursuant to California Masonic
Code Section 7040 to prepare and submit at the Annual Communication of
Grand Lodge a budget based upon the approved per capita set the previous year
by vote of the Grand Lodge.
The attached proposed Grand Lodge Budget for the period November
1, 2006 through October 31, 2007 has been carefully reviewed and
recommended by your Grand Treasurer and unanimously approved by your
Grand Lodge Finance Committee and was achieved after four full years of
comprehensive and exhaustive study of each line item. It is based on as
accurate information as is logically possible.
This Grand Lodge Budget is funded by a per capita of $27.50 and is
based on a membership of 65,382 Master Masons together with income from
fees, interest and a Grant from the California Masonic Foundation of $113,500
(from designated funds received from the Brethren from our yearly philanthropy
campaign) to fund our increasing efforts to provide Masonic Education and
Leadership Training. Any such grant funds not used will be returned to the
Foundation.
For the 2006-2007 Masonic year we will continue to develop our
Lodge Management Certification Programs and add an online training
component permitting completion at home at the convenience of the Brethren.
New DVD supplements to our new degree handbooks will permit ALL Masons
to study our Craft's principles and examine the meaning of our Degrees and
applicability to their daily lives through in-home use. Masonry in California is
reaching out to use 21st Century communication media.
Recognizing the importance of Inspectors, we will be asking ALL
Inspectors to attend a 1½ day Inspectors Seminar paid by Grand Lodge to assist
them in their duties and bring them up to date with regard to information being
taught at our Wardens Retreats.
Approximately seventy percent (70%) of our per capita income has
been designated to be spent on services to members, Lodges and Inspectors.
This year Grand Lodge expenses in some areas have not received a cost of
living increase, while others have increased slightly over last year. In several
expense line items the amount of contributions by other Masonic entities in
sharing of “in-common” expense items has been re-assessed resulting in less
contributions from those entities thereby increasing the Grand Lodge's
contribution (including Grand Lodge rent of $26,500 to the California Masonic

66

PROCEEDINGS OF THE

Memorial Temple entity as Grand Lodge's fair rental value; and $15,000 less
contribution from other Masonic entities to our Annual Communication).
The salaries of the Grand Secretary and Assistant Grand Secretary are
set forth under General and Administrative expense. The figure in the right
hand column is the net figure supported by the per capita. Since these two
positions are responsible as administrators for all Masonic corporate entities in
California, the cost is off-set in large part by payment from other Masonic
entities. It should be noted that there are a total of 7.5 full time equivalent
persons working directly for the Grand Lodge to support our Lodges and 68,000
members.
It is my pleasure to present to you a balanced Budget which will
sustain our efforts to fulfill the promise made to every applicant for the Degrees:
to make good men into exemplary members of their family and community.

PROCEEDINGS OF THE

INSTALLATION ADDRESS
MELVYN B. STEIN
Grand Master of Masons in California, 2006-2007
It is my honor and privilege that you are here today for this installation
ceremony, and even a greater honor to be permitted to serve our Craft as Grand
Master for this new Masonic year.
In the middle of the 1940’s, a radio commentator named Gabriel
Heater, would begin his news program with the words”: “There’s good news
tonight”!
This year we will begin with the same words: There’s good news
tonight, and Masonry is on the move.
Those of you who have been with us for the last several days have seen
the beginning of a new program of Masonic Education, using DVD’s to tell
applicants, families and friends in their homes what Masonry is and what it
stands for. Your Grand Lodge has commenced the training of Master Masons
by special certification programs to impart to our lodges and members how
Masonry can be applied in our daily lives, and the meaning and significance of
how the Craft will benefit our family, flag and fraternity.
Three new Masonic proficiency handbooks, available to all, explain the
meaning and significance of many portions of our three degrees to Masons as
well as our families. We must proceed beyond just conferring the three degrees
and trusting that someday a Mason will learn its true meaning. The lodge room
is a representation of the world, and when the candidate enters through the two
columns, he is reminded of King Solomon’s Temple as well as the fact that the
interior of the lodge represents the world. As the candidate follows the Senior
Deacon and places his trust in him, a brother learns how to deal with the many
trials and tribulations in life as he proceeds through the degrees. It is time for us
to unveil the principles that we are taught when we obligate ourselves to our
family, flag and fraternity while kneeling at the Altar before the Holy Writings.
Within the last several years a major influx of young men have joined
our fraternity seeking this information and they are entitled to much of it
without lifelong study.
We recognize the importance of imparting Masonic truths to our
brethren in order that they may know what Masonry teaches. This will enable
them to explain the significance of Masonry to every member of the community
and understand the importance of his wife, his family, his children, his country
and his brethren in Freemasonry.
The general public is continually being exposed to the mysteries of
Masonry by television, movies (such as Dan Brown’s works), websites and

GRAND LODGE F.& A.M. OF CALIFORNIA

75

media. Each Mason should be able to respond in a 20 second reply (the length
of an elevator ride to the 5th floor) to the enquiry: “What is Masonry”? There is
no time to be philosophical or explain the ritual or degrees. In the past we have
replied that it is a fraternity “to make good men better”; such a phrase has
significance to Masons, but usually means little or nothing to a non-Mason. If
we refer to the first paragraph of our strategic plan which is on the website as a
beginning point, Freemasonry can be defined easily within 20 seconds as a
men’s fraternal organization teaching that each man can improve himself by his
own good conduct toward his family, his country and all mankind. The “golden
rule” is a basic tenet. Clearly, a memorization of such a definition is not
required, but a brother should be ready to give a knowledgeable response when
confronted with the necessity of giving a 20 second reply.
The Grand Master’s emblem this year has substantial significance to
our Craft. It emphasizes the responsibility of devotion first to family, then to
flag and then to fraternity. The emphasis of these three words is displayed in
the emblem by the silhouette of the family with a background of red, white and
blue, denoting patriotism to the flag of our Country, with the symbol of
Masonry in its traditional blue background.
There is additional meaning to this emblem. It is in the form of a
shield which represents confidence, boldness and protection; while the laurel
wreath symbolizes honor, success and achievement.
The term “family” is used not only to refer to wives, widows and
children of Freemasons, it also denotes the “Family of Freemasonry” through its
appendant and concordant organizations and youth orders which should
cooperate, enhance and extend the hand of fraternalism to each other.
After 43 years in Masonry and 31 years of service on Grand Lodge
committees, it is clear that the purpose of our Grand Lodge and goal of its
leadership must be to serve the lodges and their membership. We, as part of
Grand Lodge, will assist in any way reasonably possible to assure that Masonry
is known throughout our community for its high moral standards.
There is good news tonight as Masonry has received more applicants
and performed more degrees in the last year than yearly in the last preceding 23
years.
We are on the move; starting tomorrow, your elected Grand Lodge line
will meet with six key committee chairmen to set goals and objectives to serve
our lodges and brethren. On Friday, your Executive Committee will meet with
the Grand Lecturer and the Assistant Grand Lecturers to improve
communications to better serve the Inspectors and their constituent lodges. Our
newly elected Junior Grand Warden has spent the last eight months meeting
with your elected Grand Lodge Officers and will hit the ground running
tomorrow.

76

PROCEEDINGS OF THE

Next month your Inspectors will attend a day and a half seminar (one
in the North and one in the South) which has been redesigned within the last
several months to bring them the information that has been provided to the
Wardens at their Retreats and at the Lodge Certification Programs in addition to
other substantial information. I recognize the importance of our Inspectors, who
are the key to our individual brethren, through our lodge structure.
Our philanthropy campaign will continue to assist our Masonic
Education program; the California Masonic Homes Outreach Program reaching
into the homes of our widows and brethren who need help and assistance; the
Masonic Foundation providing scholarships and assisting in supervision of the
child identification program. This year the Grand Master’s special project will
focus on the protection and preservation and exhibition and traveling
presentations throughout California of the large number of Masonic historical
artifacts held by your Grand Lodge. As an example, this white Grand Master’s
apron is but one of the aprons and matching collars with the golden California
bear embroidered thereon used by your Grand Lodge around 1911. When
Masonry came to California with the gold rush in 1848, and for more than 150
years Masons have been a vital part of the history of this state. Our Grand
Lodge library and museum has a large number of donated items, including a
lambskin apron said to have been made by a continental soldier at Valley Forge,
and another alleged to be Davy Crocket’s apron; several 150 year old hand
painted hanging trestle boards as well as those intended to be placed on the floor
are within our archives; pamphlets and photographs of the 1917 departure of the
All Member Masonic Ambulance Corps departing to Europe during the First
World War need to be protected and preserved, as well as numerous volumes of
books over 200 years old; hand signed letters from President Abraham Lincoln,
President Grant’s appointment of a brother as Post Master General of the United
States and President Truman’s letter accompanying a stone from the foundation
of the first White House; gold Masonic watches over 140 years old are just a
few of the innumerable artifacts in our collection. Our California Masonic
Foundation which oversees our Masonic library and museum has agreed to
accept special project funds of the Grand Master and restrict their use for the
maintenance, preservation, exhibition at Grand Lodge and traveling to lodges
throughout California for presentations relating to education concerning our
Masonic history. We also look forward to obtaining an online searchable index
of the library museum holdings and a display of our artifacts online by both
photographs and written articles. The key that unlocks our future is the
understanding of our past.
This coming year several of our Grand Lodge committees have been
entirely reorganized and we all look forward with enthusiasm to the continuing
challenges for improvement, while maintaining the necessary consistency of
programs over the next several years.

GRAND LODGE F.& A.M. OF CALIFORNIA

77

I look forward to serving our fraternity to the best of my ability;
keeping focused at all times in doing that which is in the best interest of our
Craft.
Masonry is on the move in California, and there is good news tonight,
as we continue to serve Family, Flag and Fraternity.

78

PROCEEDINGS OF THE

APPENDIX C

GRAND LODGE F.& A.M. OF CALIFORNIA

81

REPORT OF THE SENIOR GRAND WARDEN
Per Capita Recommendation for 2007-2008
Richard W. Hopper, Senior Grand Warden

It is my responsibility as Senior Grand Warden to propose a per capita
at the 2006 Annual Communication to provide funds to meet the 2007/2008
Grand Lodge Budget. Substantial time and effort has already been expended in
a line by line review and projection of membership and expenses in reaching the
budget.
The present system of budget projection is both comprehensive and
exhaustive and begins a full four years before a budget is recommended to
Grand Lodge at the Annual Communication. The proposed budget is carefully
tracked and adjusted as each year unfolds. That means that the budget upon
which the per capita is based is as accurate in forecasting our needs as is
logically possible.
Accordingly, a budget for 2007/2008 has been developed which will
support current and proposed programs and which reflects our projected
membership. That budget will require a per capita of $29.00. The Grand
Lodge Finance Committee, Grand Secretary, Assistant Grand Secretary, Deputy
Grand Master, Grand Treasurer and Director of Finance have met at length with
me to review the budget projections for 2007/2008, and all of us agreed that this
is the amount that is needed to support our ongoing programs.
Financial pages and graphs are attached showing that approximately
two-thirds (2/3) of the proposed per capita has been designated to be spent on
services to members, lodges and Inspectors. The per capita considers a
reduction in membership while at the same time increases expenses less than 3%
for cost of living.
Strong, active lodges are necessary to assure the success of the
strategic plans of both the Grand Lodge and individual lodges as well as attract
and retain quality members. Much of the 2007/2008 Budget is committed to
maintaining our present course in supporting membership development,
membership retention, leadership training, and Masonic education. The
proposed per capita of $29.00 will meet the 2007/2008 Grand Lodge balanced
budget requirements.
I look forward to working with the leadership and members of your
lodge as Masonry continues to be a strong and positive force for the benefit of
our brethren and our communities. With this budget, we can continue to say
that in California “Freemasonry is Standing Strong.”

REPORT OF THE GRAND TREASURER
To the Most Worshipful Grand Lodge, F.& A.M. of California:
This, the Grand Treasurer’s Report to the Grand Lodge will be brief
because of the audited financial statements of the Grand Lodge of California
and its related entities (Masonic Homes of California, the California Masonic
Foundation, the California Masonic Memorial Temple, and the Retirement Plan)
are published in their entirety in the Proceedings of this Grand Lodge. Review
these financial statements when you receive them. I am certain when you do so
you will agree with me that this, your Grand Lodge, is fiscally sound and well
managed. It is a tribute to those who have gone this way before us as well as to
the continuing dedication of the leadership of this great fraternity.
I have had the opportunity to observe many Grand Masters as they
endeavor to match financial resources with the needs of this organization; and I
can truly say to you that our leaders are dedicated to the long range future of this
organization.
When you elected me Grand Treasurer, I had the good fortune of
working with our Director of Finance and the financial services staff who,
working with my predecessor, Most Worshipful Warren J. Blomseth, had
established clear goals which maximized transparency and accountability. At
the end of the first quarter of this Grand Lodge year the Director of Finance
resigned to accept a similar position elsewhere here in San Francisco. He
departed and took with him the Controller and another long time finance staff
member. This created a serious problem in our Finance Department.
The balance of our dedicated staff carried on while a search was
launched for a new chief financial officer. Mr. Frank Tsai was hired and began
in that capacity in June, and has thus far proven to be a valued addition to the
staff. He has selected an interim Controller. While some earlier established
goals were temporarily set aside during the period of staff rebuilding, we are
now proceeding with even greater dedication.
Considerable time has been expended by staff and consultants
evaluating the retirement program for our employees. A new program will soon
be proposed which will both be more economical for the Grand Lodge and its
related entities, and will also better serve our employees.
I want to acknowledge the work of the Finance Committee of the
Grand Lodge which also functions as the Audit Committee and the Retirement
Committee. Their service this year has been very helpful and is most
appreciated.

90

PROCEEDINGS OF THE

The Investment Committee members continue to share their expertise
to benefit this Grand Lodge by their oversight of our investment portfolio for
long term growth and protection of the assets entrusted to them. This Grand
Lodge can feel confident that its investments are well managed.
Finally, my thanks to the Grand Master, the Executive Committee, the
Grand Secretary, Assistant Grand Secretary, and the entire staff of the Grand
Lodge for their dedication and support this year. My thanks to you, my
brethren, for the opportunity to serve this Grand Lodge.
Respectfully submitted,
M. William Holsinger, PGM, Grand Treasurer

APPENDIX E

GRAND LODGE F.& A.M. OF CALIFORNIA

93

REPORT OF THE GRAND SECRETARY
Most Worshipful Grand Master and Brethren – This is my fifteenth
report to you as Grand Secretary. It is a privilege to serve you as such, and a
particular privilege and pleasure to serve our 141st Grand Master, Most
Worshipful Frederick L. Sorsabal. His devotion to the Craft, and to developing
a deeper understanding of our mission as Masons, has made this year a very
special one. On behalf of our entire Grand Lodge Office Staff, I would like to
thank him for all his courtesies this year. He has made our job a much easier
one by the care for all those around him which is his hallmark.
The year began with the challenge of dealing with the theft of the
Grand Lodge Grand Officer Jewels, which was reported to you at our last
Annual Communication. Although the San Francisco Police were unable to
discover the perpetrator of this crime, and the jewels were not recovered, the
incident provided a sobering and much needed look at our security procedures at
the Grand Lodge Office. With the assistant and support of the Board of
Trustees of the California Masonic Memorial Temple, many new security
systems were installed, and new procedures adopted, to minimize the risk of a
repeat of this most unfortunate episode. I am pleased to report that the
insurance carrier paid the claim in full, less the $10,000 deductible in the policy.
We were thus able to recover $98,000 to be used toward the restoration of the
regalia of the Grand Lodge Officers, and a selected replacement of some of the
jewels which had been stolen. A complete report on this will be found in the
printed version of this report.
I am also pleased to report to you that our Member Services
Department has continued to expand its service to our lodges. Not only were
many more secretaries afforded access to the new iMember System through
training workshops, but as of this date 200 lodge secretaries have taken the
training program and are working directly in the Grand Lodge Database. Not
only does this result in less work for our lodge secretaries, but the end result is
much more accurate lodge records and Grand Lodge records.
The
Administrative Website continues to be improved, and is now a primary
communications tool for most of our lodge officers, past masters, Grand lodge
committeemen, inspectors, and Grand Lodge Officer. We have truly entered a
mature phase of the electronic era. Most of our correspondence is now via
email, thus facilitating communication and reducing the enormous paper load
that has clogged our communication processes in the past.
The Grand Secretary’s Office is responsible for handling lodge
consolidations, and this past year eight lodges consolidated into four new
lodges. Offsetting the number of consolidations was the chartering of two new
lodges, and the issuing of a dispensation to one other new lodge. Our number of
lodges now stands at 351.

94

PROCEEDINGS OF THE

We had 68,714 Master Masons on the rolls of our lodges as of June 30
of this year. Of those, 2,113 had their dues remitted, and thus there were 66,601
Master Masons for whom the lodges paid the per capita. We had 2,564 deaths
reported, and 1,072 suspended for non-payment of dues. We had 890 who took
a dimit or a discontinuation of multiple membership.
The Grand Secretary is also responsible for the Institute for Masonic
Studies, and this year the Grand Master assigned the Masonic Formation
Program to the Institute, and to the Grand Secretary. A task force of twenty
Master Masons labored to produce the Masonic Formation Certification
Program, and the Masonic Formation Manual to accompany the new program.
A blend of candidate education and lifelong Master Mason Education, Masonic
Formation has rapidly caught on as a way of teaching our members to live the
principles of Freemasonry daily. This past year several lodges sent one or more
members to be trained and certified, and it is expected that the Masonic
Formation Program will take a firm hold over the next few months as twelve
more certification workshops are offered.
One major project of the Masonic Formation Program – in cooperation
with the Grand Lodge Ritual Committee – was to develop a new multimedia
tool for use with the lectures. The lectures of our degrees are packed with
information needed to understand the basic degrees themselves, but we have not
had the advantage of the use of modern technology to assist in the effective
presentation of those lectures. That is ending. This year we created a DVD to
accompany the Lecture of the Entered Apprentice Degree – a copy of which will
be distributed through the inspectors after this Annual Communication. In order
for you to see a small part of what has been developed, we will now view the
multimedia materials to be used with the Second Section of the Lecture of the
Entered Apprentice Degree. Worshipful Bro. Robert D. Rowan, Assistant
Grand Lecturer for Division I, will give that part of the lecture now – with the
new multimedia presentation as the background. This material was designed
not only for use with the lecture itself, but with candidate education that follows
the degree. Please give your attention to Worshipful Bro. Bob Rowan:
[multimedia and lecture begin].
I wish to especially thank Worshipful Bro. Adam Kendall, Master of
Phoenix Lodge No. 144, and the Curator of the Grand Lodge Library/Museum,
for his assistance with this project, and Brother Paul Adams, a member of
Oakland Durant Rockridge Lodge No. 188, who worked with Wor. Bro. Rowan
to bring this multimedia DVD about. We expect it to be tested this year in our
lodges, with valuable feedback expected, so that a final version can be released
early next year.
Masonic Formation, which is a commitment to empower our
candidates and members to truly understand the teachings of Freemasonry, and
apply them daily in their lives, is a major undertaken. We could not have
accomplished what we have accomplished without the generous support of the

GRAND LODGE F.& A.M. OF CALIFORNIA

95

Craft – especially financially – as they responded so strongly to the Grand
Master’s appeal this year. It is evident that a significant number of our members
believe in the future of Freemasonry, and are willing to step up to the plate to
make it a reality. The responsibility of the Masonic Formation Task Force is to
produce a program worthy of this support, and I truly believe that you will see
better and better things next year, and in the years to follows.
The printed report in the Proceedings will include many more things
than can be mentioned here, but I would be remiss if I did not tell you that our
California Freemason magazine, and our public website, freemason.org,
continue to win top honors in nationwide competition. Our Assistant Grand
Secretary, Worshipful Allan Casalou, is the man responsible for all this, and I
would like to acknowledge his significant achievements in this –and in so many
other areas – for your Grand Lodge. We are most fortunate to have his
leadership available to us!
Most Worshipful, this concludes my fifteenth report to Grand Lodge.
Respectfully submitted,
John L. Cooper III, Grand Secretary

APPENDIX F
