If Resolution No. 06-05 is adopted, the following changes
shall be made to the Fourth Edition of the California Masonic Code.

Section 804.690 of the Code would be replaced by the following:

§804.690. FORFEITURE OF FEES AND CONTRIBUTIONS.
When a candidate does not present himself qualified for the Fellow Craft and
Master Mason Degrees within a period of three years from the date of his
initiation as an Entered Apprentice, the Master shall appoint a committee of two
or more members of the Lodge to investigate and report back to the Master the
reasons why the candidate has not presented himself for advancement. If the
candidate does not supply any explanation satisfactory to the Lodge, the Master
shall direct the Secretary to forward to such candidate either by registered letter
or certified mail, with return receipt requested, deposited in the U.S. Post Office,
addressed to him at his last known address, a notice advising him that unless he
shall present himself qualified for advancement, on or before a specified date (at
least 60 days in advance), his fees for the unconferred degrees and all of his
previous contributions shall be declared forfeited.

GRAND LODGE F.& A.M. OF CALIFORNIA

165

On the date specified, the Master shall declare the fees and contributions
forfeited unless further time shall have been granted by a vote of the Lodge.
A candidate thus denied advancement shall be considered only as an Entered
Apprentice or Fellow Craft of the Lodge with the right to apply for the
remaining degree or degrees, and again paying the fees and contributions
therefor, subject to investigation and election, the same as any other candidate.

If Resolution No. 06-06 is adopted, the following changes
shall be made to the Fourth Edition of the California Masonic Code.

Section 402.030 of the Code would be amended to read as marked:

§402.030. CIPHER RITUALS.
Grand Lodge shall cause to be prepared, published and sold to Constituent
Lodges and Master Masons, for use and possession by Master Masons only, a
cipher of the Ritual, in any medium capable of being rendered into clearly
legible tangible form, as shall be approved from time to time by the Grand
Lecturer with the approval of the Grand Master. The cipher may include such
attached or separate supplements as shall be approved from time to time by the
Grand Lecturer with the approval of the Grand Master.
Grand Lodge shall also cause to be prepared, published and sold to Master
Masons only, the lectures (including the Middle Chamber Lecture) in a separate
booklet with a bare minimum of key words in abbreviated form or in cipher, as
shall be approved from time to time by the Grand Lecturer with the approval of
the Grand Master.
The foregoing notwithstanding, a Master Mason who is an authorized
Candidate’s Coach may reproduce that portion of the approved cipher ritual that
pertains to the Entered Apprentice and Fellow Craft proficiency examinations
for use and possession by an Entered Apprentice or Fellow Craft to prepare for
his proficiency examination.
In no event shall any cipher ritual, or portion thereof, be used during a tiled
meeting except where such meeting is called for the purpose of practicing the
Ritual. Such use, however, shall be permitted only by members who are not
participating in the presentation of the Ritual.

Section 815.000 of the Code would be amended to read as marked:

§815.000. GRAND MASTER’S LODGE.
The Grand Master’s Lodge is a special classification of Constituent Lodge
which shall be subject to all provisions of this Code applicable to Constituent
Lodges, except as follows:
A.

There shall be only one Grand Master’s Lodge in this Jurisdiction, and
it shall be chartered with the name “Grand Master’s Lodge” without a
Lodge number;

B.

The officers of the Grand Master’s Lodge shall be the Grand Master as
Master, the Senior Grand Warden as Senior Warden, the Junior Grand
Warden as Junior Warden, the Grand Treasurer as Treasurer, and the
Grand Secretary as Secretary, each of whom shall serve in this office
ex officio;

C.

Membership shall consist only of:
1.

As ex-officio members, the Grand Master, the Deputy Grand
Master, the Senior Grand Warden, the Junior Grand Warden,
the Grand Treasurer and the Grand Secretary; and

2.

Those Entered Apprentices, Fellow Crafts and Master Masons
of a Constituent Lodge of this Jurisdiction at the time that the
Lodge’s charter is surrendered or revoked who are not at the
time a plural or dual member in another Lodge;

D.

Membership in the Grand Master’s Lodge shall cease for any member
upon taking a Demit therefrom or by a plural or dual membership in
another Lodge;

E.

The Grand Master’s Lodge shall hold no meetings nor confer any
degrees but may request courtesy work for its Entered Apprentices and
Fellow Crafts in another Lodge;

F.

The provisions of this Code requiring certain recommendations,
notifications and certificates from an Inspector dealing with
qualifications of officers and membership in a Lodge seeking a
Dispensation or Charter shall not apply to the Grand Master’s Lodge.
Its Dispensation and Charter shall be issued without charge or vote of
Grand Lodge;

G.

The forms of Petition for Dispensation, Petition for Charter, Charter
and Uniform Code of Bylaws shall be adopted insofar as the provisions

GRAND LODGE F.& A.M. OF CALIFORNIA

167

may be applicable to the Grand Master’s Lodge, except as required to
comply with the provisions of this section;
H.

The provisions of this Code dealing with the qualifications of the
Master, Senior Warden and Junior Warden of a Constituent Lodge
shall not apply to the officers of the Grand Master’s Lodge;

I.

The Grand Master’s Lodge shall not be entitled to representation in
Grand Lodge or any vote therein;

J.

Dues for the operation of the Grand Master’s Lodge shall be
established by the Grand Master each year in an amount sufficient to
offset any cost to Grand Lodge for the operation of the Grand Master’s
Lodge, imposing no burden on the per capita. These dues shall be
sufficient not only to pay the costs of the operation of the Grand
Master’s Lodge, but also the per capita due Grand Lodge each year;
and

K.

The life members of a constituent Lodge at the time that the Lodge’s
charter is surrendered or revoked who become members of the Grand
Master’s Lodge shall be life members of the Grand Master’s Lodge and
shall be forever exempt from the payment of dues in the Grand
Master’s Lodge. Any other provision of this Code to the contrary
notwithstanding, no such life member shall be entitled to payment of
his interest in the Life Membership Fund of the dissolved Lodge. His
interest in the Life Membership Fund of the dissolved Lodge shall be
kept by Grand Lodge in a separate fund, and the interest or dividends
paid in cash on such fund shall be used to offset any cost to Grand
Lodge for the operation of the Grand Master’s Lodge. If his
membership in the Grand Master’s Lodge shall cease for any reason,
his life membership in the Grand Master’s Lodge shall cease also; and

L.K.

All other actions requiring the consent or vote of the Lodge shall be
effected by the Grand Master acting alone.

§806.000. COMMITTEES.
A.

The Master and Wardens shall be a Charity Committee, and shall have

time provide by a standing resolution), for the relief of a distressed

for the relief of a member in good standing (other than a senior or

B.

The Master, at the Stated Meeting next succeeding his installation,
shall appoint an Auditing Committee, whose duty it shall be to examine
all accounts presented against the Lodge and the books and records of
the Treasurer and Secretary. Neither the Treasurer nor Secretary may
serve on the Audit Committee.

C.

The Master, at the Stated Meeting next succeeding his installation,
shall appoint a Delinquent Dues Committee to personally, or otherwise,
interview each delinquent member, ascertain the cause, and work
closely with the Master and officers in a continuing effort to effect the
retention of all deserving members unable to pay.

D.

The Lodge may have such other committees as the Master or the Lodge
may deem proper to appoint. All committees shall be appointed by the
Master, unless the Lodge provides otherwise.

E.

All reports of committees shall be in writing.

Section 812.010 of the Code would be amended to read as marked:

§812.010. GENERALLY.
It is the duty of every Lodge to aid and assist every needy Mason of that Lodge,
wherever he may reside, and every needy Mason of another Lodge in this or any
other Jurisdiction who resides within the vicinity of that Lodge. This duty
extends to the needy widow and dependant orphans of a Master Mason of that
Lodge and the needy widow and dependant orphans of a Master Mason of
another Lodge in this or any other Jurisdiction who reside within the vicinity of
that Lodge. A Lodge’s duty of relief is limited only by its own circumstances
and ability.
It is the duty of every Lodge to aid and assist every needy member in good
standing (other than a senior or majority member) of any of the Masonic Youth
Orders who resides within the vicinity of that Lodge, according to the Lodge’s
own circumstances and ability.
For purposes of this Code, a “widow” is one who was married to a Master
Mason at the time of his death. A widow who remarries forfeits all rights to
Masonic relief from her previous husband pursuant to this section, even though
such remarriage is later terminated by divorce, dissolution, or otherwise.
For purposes of this Code, an “orphan” is the son or daughter under the age of

GRAND LODGE F.& A.M. OF CALIFORNIA

169

majority of a deceased Master Mason. No man or woman over the age of
majority shall be entitled to relief as a matter of right. However, a Lodge may,
in its discretion, provide relief in exceptional circumstances to the adult child of
a deceased Master Mason where a need arises by reason of a physical or mental
impairment.
Money given a Mason or his family as relief by a Lodge cannot be charged to
him.

AMENDMENTS

Section 1. Those portions of these Bylaws relating to the day and time of Stated
Meetings and the amount of fees and dues may be amended at any Stated
Meeting by a two-thirds vote of the members present. All proposed amendment
must be presented, in writing, at a preceding Stated Meeting and written notice
must given to each Lodge member setting forth the text of the proposed
amendment and the date of the Stated Meeting at which the vote is to be taken.
It shall have no effect until approved by the Grand Master. The Secretary of the
Lodge shall note in the minutes of the Lodge the notice of the proposed
amendments when made. When transmitting an amendment for approval, he
shall forward a certificate, under seal, showing that the law has been complied
with and that the amendment has received the required vote.

Report of the Committee on Policy and General Purposes:
The Grand Master recommends that the delegates to this Grand Lodge
adopt a proposed new Code of Masonic Laws.
A complete revision of our Code is an enormous and vitally important
undertaking, and those who are working on this project are to be commended
for this ambitious assignment. But, to expect a fully completed and polished
product in little more than the six months in which the Code Revision
Committee has worked, is to expect too much. A review of the proposed new

172

PROCEEDINGS OF THE

Code has found there to be numerous errors as well as changes, errors and
changes which may be inadvertent, but nevertheless amount to substantive
changes in the law. It is irrelevant why the proposed revision results in changes
in our law. The problem is that changes do exist as the proposed Code is now
drafted. There is no reason for us to rush this vitally important project.
In his Recommendation, Grand Master Sorsabal states that “No doubt
this [revision] process will result in some changes having been made when new
language replaces old language.” While that may be true, there are several
instances in the proposed new Code which are far more than mere “changes in
language.” In some cases, existing time limits have been changed, and in
others, time limits were inserted where none had previously existed. Some of
the text of the current Code has simply been deleted, thereby changing the entire
meaning of the law. Other changes directly affect responsibilities placed on our
constituent Lodges.
Before this Recommendation can be properly evaluated by the Craft,
all revisions in the proposed Code which change the current law should be
enumerated so the Craft can make an intelligent decision on whether to adopt
the proposed new Code.
Our past experience has demonstrated that it has taken from two to four
years to prepare a quality finished Code revision that is ready for adoption.
There is no need for haste or a rush to judgment on this project. Our current
Code has served us well for the past 15 years. The overriding principle
governing the preparation of a complete Code revision should be accuracy, not
haste.
The proposed Code revision, as presented, forms a good basis from
which a final version for future adoption may be crafted. However, as it now
stands, the proposed revision is clearly a work in progress, not a finished
product.
Our past policy has been, when presenting a revised Code for adoption,
to take the time necessary to prepare a precise and accurate version. The
proposed Code revision the Grand Master now recommends does not meet that
test.
This recommendation is not consistent with the policies and purposes
of this Grand Lodge.

GRAND LODGE F.& A.M. OF CALIFORNIA

173

Report of the Committee on Finance:
The Finance Committee estimates that the cost of producing and
printing new copies of the California Masonic Code will be fully recovered by
sales of the Code through the Grand Lodge Supply Room. The price of the
Code will be determined by the printing and production costs. Electronic copies
will be made available at no cost on the Administrative Website and since the
Code has already been developed in electronic format there is no cost to post it
on the website.
Recommendation ADOPTED.
Grand Master Sorsabal’s Recommendation No. 3
Date for Presentation for Diamond Jubilee Award
Masons who live long enough to become eligible for the Diamond
Jubilee Award are, by definition, advanced in years. Until very recently a man
had to be twenty-one years of age to petition for the degrees of Masonry, and
simple arithmetic will show you that a Mason would have to be at least 96 years
of age to be eligible for a seventy-five year Diamond Jubilee Award. Many are
older.
With advancing years men become more frail, and life is fraught with
increasing health problems in all too many cases. Over the years Grand Masters
have realized that many men who will become eligible to receive the Diamond
Jubilee Award from them never make it. The sands of time run out, and we lose
the opportunity to present the Diamond Jubilee Award to an otherwise worthy
brother Master Mason. As a result, I propose that we change our law to permit
the presentation of the Diamond Jubilee Award to any otherwise eligible Master
Mason who is in good standing during the calendar year in which he will
become eligible, having paid his dues for the current year, or having had them
duly remitted by vote of the lodge. That gives the Grand Master greater
flexibility in scheduling the presentation of the award at a time when the lodge
and the family can be present, and eases somewhat the rigorous standard which
presently prevails under which there are no exceptions to the inflexible rule that
a Master Mason must have been such for seventy-five full years before the
award can be presented.
I realize that nothing will be perfect, and that even this flexibility will
not address all the issues surrounding the presentation of these awards. But it
will address most of them. If you agree, I urge you to vote to change our law to
allow the Diamond Jubilee Award to be presented any time during the calendar
year in which the eligibility is achieved.
If you adopt this recommendation, Section 23150 of the Code would be
amended to read as marked:

174

PROCEEDINGS OF THE

§23150. GOLDEN VETERAN AND DIAMOND JUBILEE AWARDS.
The Grand Lodge, upon request, shall provide a Golden Veteran’s Award to
members of this Jurisdiction who have been in good standing for a total of 50
years or more, presentation being made by the Grand Master, District Inspector,
or their representative with appropriate ceremony.
The Grand Lodge, upon request, shall provide a Diamond Jubilee Award to
members of this Jurisdiction who have been in good standing for a total of 75
years or more, presentation being made by the Grand Master or his designee
with appropriate ceremony. The presentation of the Diamond Jubilee Award
may be made at any time during the calendar year in which the member shall
become eligible.
In the case of a deceased Mason, if he was eligible for but had not received the
Award prior to his death, or in the case of an incompetent Brother, presentation
on request may be made to the family or heirs.
In computing the length of time requisite for issuance of either award, a period
of up to one year, during which the Brother held a Dimit from any Lodge
recognized by this Grand Lodge, may be included; however, periods during
which the Brother held a Dimit for more than one year from the date of the
Dimit, or was suspended for non-payment of dues, must be added to the time
when the Brother would otherwise have been eligible to receive the award. The
period between the issuance of a Dimit for the purpose of becoming a charter
member of a new Lodge and the institution of the new Lodge is not an
interruption of the required number of years of continuous membership.

Report of the Committee on Policy and General Purposes:
Current California Masonic Code section 23150 provides, in part, that
a man must be a member of a Lodge for 75 years, or more, before he is eligible
to receive the Diamond Jubilee award. This law always has been interpreted to
mean that the recipient becomes eligible once that 75 year period has been fully
completed. The Grand Master recommends that section 23150 be amended so
that the Diamond Jubilee award could be presented at any time during the year
the recipient becomes eligible, even though he might have served our Craft for a
few months short of 75 years.
Presently, members who reach this milestone would be a minimum of
96 years old during their year of eligibility of age. Most men that age may not
be in the best of health. This change would allow the Grand Lodge and the
Grand Master to approach these men at an earlier date in the year allowing the
recipient and his family to have more time and opportunity to prepare for this
most prestigious acknowledgement of their dedication and commitment to this
great craft.

GRAND LODGE F.& A.M. OF CALIFORNIA

175

These men have lived well beyond their normal life expectancies; it
makes sense to provide them every opportunity to receive this auspicious
recognition. Passage of this Recommendation will provide, even if for just a
few, the right to receive the Diamond Jubilee Award at a time when they may be
counting the rest of their life in days or weeks, not years.
This recommendation is consistent with the policies and general
purpose of this Grand Lodge.

Report of the Committee on Finance:
The Finance Committee has reviewed Recommendation No. 3 and
reports the following.
Each pin costs the Grand Lodge $70.00. This year there were 20
eligible recipients at the beginning of the year but seven died before the pin
could be presented. If all 20 had been presented it would have cost Grand
Lodge $1,400 instead of $980. As the number of members drops there will of
course be fewer eligible for the pines and therefore the cost will drop as well.
However, based on this year’s information, this recommendation would have
cost Grand Lodge an additional $420 – a very small amount of the per capita.

Recommendation ADOPTED.
Grand Master Sorsabal’s Recommendation No. 4
Eligibility of Applicants from Military Families
At present we may accept an application from a man who is “A
member of any uniformed service of the United States, whose duty station is at a
facility or on a vessel having an officially designated home port….” for six
months preceding the application – instead of the year of residence in California
required of all other applicants. This residency of six months may be waived
upon application by vote of the lodge for a dispensation, which can then be
granted by the Inspector. However neither of these provisions addresses the
situation where an applicant has resided in California less than a year because
his wife is with the uniformed services. I propose changing our law to permit a
man to apply for the degrees of Masonry in one of our lodges when his wife has
been on duty at a facility or on a vessel having an officially designated home
port in California for six months preceding, or – with a proper application by the
lodge for a dispensation – for less than six months. In other words, in addition
to the exception for the applicant himself, an exception would be made for an
applicant whose residency of less than one year has been caused by his wife’s
service to our country in the uniformed service of the United States. Suitable

176

PROCEEDINGS OF THE

language to accomplish this follows. If you agree, I hope that you will support
this amendment to our code.
If you adopt this recommendation, the following changes will be made
to the Code. Section 4030 of the Code would be amended to read as marked:
§4030. POWER OF INSPECTOR TO GRANT DISPENSATIONS.
Upon a written request from a Lodge after the required vote, the Inspector, on
behalf of the Grand Master, may grant dispensations for:
A.

Holding elections for a Lodge officer or officers at times other than the
prescribed time, except for the purpose of filling a vacancy, in which
case no dispensation is required. Application for each dispensation
shall be by the Lodge after a majority vote by ballot;

B.

Changing the date and/or time of a Stated Meeting on such notice to
the members of the Lodge as the Inspector may require. Application
for each dispensation shall be by the Lodge after majority vote by
ballot;

C.

Receiving and acting upon applications for degrees of men belonging a
man who belongs or whose spouse belongs to the armed forces a
uniformed service of the United States who does not possess the
qualification of residence. The Lodge shall give at least 10 days
written notice to each of its members of the meeting at which a ballot
on any application for such dispensation will be taken and the purpose
thereof. Application for each dispensation shall be by the Lodge after
unanimous vote by ballot.
If the dispensation is issued, the application for degrees shall be
processed as provided for elsewhere in this Code for regular
applications;

D.

The wearing of Masonic Clothing and Masonic Regalia, including
station aprons, officers’ jewels, and Masters’ top hats, in public
processions and parades. Application for each dispensation shall be by
the Lodge after majority vote by ballot; and

E.

Approval of a building other than that fixed in the Lodge Charter or
Dispensation, or place in the open for a Special Meeting of a Lodge.

Section 26040 of the Code would be amended to read as marked:

§26040. UNIFORMED SERVICES RESIDENCE.
A.

A member of any uniformed service of the United States, whose duty
station is at a facility or on a vessel having an officially designated
home port, may apply for and receive the degrees of Masonry in any
Lodge having jurisdiction of the location of such facility or home port.
If the member of any such uniformed service has not resided at such
facility or been attached to such vessel for at least six months

B.

A man residing with his spouse who is a member of any uniformed

Guard, the U.S. Public Health Service and the National Oceanic and
Atmospheric Administration.

Section 26390 of the Code would be amended to read as marked:

§26390. EXCLUSIVE JURISDICTION TO CONFER DEGREES.
A Lodge shall not recognize as a Mason or confer a courtesy degree upon any
person who, at a time when he was a resident of this Jurisdiction, was elected or
claims to have been elected to receive the degrees of Masonry from any source
beyond this Jurisdiction without proper permission, until a Lodge of this
Jurisdiction shall have received him as a member.
The proper permission may only be given by the Lodge nearest his place of
residence, and in the manner necessary for an election to receive the degrees. A
resident of this Jurisdiction who receives one or more of the degrees elsewhere
and then applies for the remaining degrees to the Lodge within whose
Jurisdiction he resides, is not required to pay again for the degree or degrees
already received and may tender with his application only that portion of the
fees attributable to the remaining degree or degrees.
A Lodge shall recognize as a Mason a person who, while he or his spouse was
in the uniformed service of the United States, has received the degrees of

178

PROCEEDINGS OF THE

Masonry in a regular Lodge under a Jurisdiction recognized by this Jurisdiction
in which he or his spouse was is stationed at the time of receiving the degrees
and in accordance with the laws of that Jurisdiction.

Report of the Committee on Policy and General Purposes:
California Masonic Code section 26040 provides that a member of any
uniformed service of the United States, whose duty station is at a facility or on a
vessel having an officially designated home port, may apply for and receive the
degrees of Masonry in any Lodge having jurisdiction of the location of such
facility or home port. This Recommendation seeks to allow the husband of a
woman who has been called to active duty the same benefit. The husband is
considered a dependant, by military standards, and travels with his wife
wherever she may be stationed, war zones excluded. The shoe is just on the
other foot so to speak.
It is the policy of this Grand Lodge to support our men and women in
uniform, as well as the spouses of our men and women in uniform. This
Recommendation would best serve the men who are, one way or the other,
attached to the military, and are desirous of becoming a Mason. The same
consideration should be given to the spouse of a woman who is in uniformed
service to our country as is currently provided for those men who actually wear
the uniform.
This recommendation is consistent with the policies and general
purposes of this Grand Lodge.

Recommendation ADOPTED.
Grand Master Sorsabal’s Recommendation No. 5
Fiscal Year of Grand Lodge and Grand Lodge Financial Statements
Section 2030 of the California Masonic Code requires the financial
statements of Grand Lodge to be prepared on the basis of a fiscal year ending
with the month in which the Annual Communication is held. This language was
placed in the code when the Annual Communication was always held in the
month of October, beginning on the Second Monday. The code was amended
year before last to permit the Annual Communication to be held within twenty
days before or after the Second Monday in October. The adoption of this
flexibility has created a problem for the fiscal year of Grand Lodge by causing
confusion as to when the fiscal year will actually end. If the Annual
Communication is in September, the fiscal year could be one month short. If it
is held in November it could be one month longer. Such flexibility does not
work for accounting purposes. I therefore propose that the code be amended to

GRAND LODGE F.& A.M. OF CALIFORNIA

179

provide that the financial statements of Grand Lodge are to be prepared on the
basis of a fiscal year ending with the month of October. That will keep our
existing practice, and eliminate confusion as to when the fiscal year will close
each year.
If you adopt this recommendation, Section 2030 of the Code would be amended
to read as marked:
§2030. GRAND TREASURER.
It shall be the duty of the Grand Treasurer:
A.

To receive all monies belonging to Grand Lodge from the Grand
Secretary, giving his receipt therefor, and, immediately upon the
receipt of said monies, to deposit the same to the credit of Grand
Lodge, in a bank or banks selected by the Grand Treasurer with the
approval of the Grand Master. Funds on deposit shall be withdrawn by
the Grand Treasurer, or in case of his inability or unavailability, by the
Grand Master or Deputy Grand Master and the Grand Secretary;

B.

Keep an accurate, complete and uniform set of financial records,
accounting for the receipts, disbursements and properly recognizable
assets and liabilities of Grand Lodge and the various entities of the
Grand Lodge. Such records shall be kept on the accrual basis of
accounting and maintained in accordance with generally accepted
accounting principles;

C.

Take charge of all other property of Grand Lodge, except property
specifically charged to the Grand Secretary, and to keep an accurate
account thereof. Bonds and other securities held in the name of the
respective nominees of the several entities of Grand Lodge, shall be
deposited with a bank or trust company or in safe deposit vaults,
selected as provided in subdivision “A” of this section. Such securities
shall be withdrawn from such depository only on the written order of
the Grand Treasurer, and in case of bearer securities only on the written
order of the Grand Treasurer approved by the Grand Master, Deputy
Grand Master, Senior Grand Warden or Junior Grand Warden, or in
case of the absence or inability of the Grand Treasurer to act, then as to
any security only on the written order of the Grand Secretary approved
by any one the following: the Grand Master, Deputy Grand Master, the
Senior Grand Warden or Junior Grand Warden;

D.

Make transfers of funds under such regulations as may be provided by
Grand Lodge; to disburse funds for all appropriations by Grand Lodge,
for all orders of the Grand Master to meet cases of emergency that may
arise in a recess of Grand Lodge, and in payment of bills, claims and
demands upon Grand Lodge; and to audit and approve claims for
reimbursement of expenses submitted as provided herein;

180

PROCEEDINGS OF THE

E.

Attend Grand Lodge at all its Communications, except Ceremonial
Communications, and to make available to the Grand Master, when
required, the books and all necessary records appertaining to his office;
and also, if required by Grand Lodge or the Grand Master, to provide
such books and records to any committee which may be appointed to
act in relation to the fiscal affairs of Grand Lodge;

F.

Prepare and deliver, at each Annual Communication, a detailed report
of the financial condition of Grand Lodge and all related entities
prepared in accordance with generally accepted accounting principles.
Such report shall have been examined by the independent public
accountant of Grand Lodge, submitted to the Committee on Finance,
and shall be published in the Proceedings of Grand Lodge. The
financial statements of Grand Lodge shall be prepared on a fiscal year
ending with the end of the calendar month during which the Annual
Communication is held October 31st. The financial statements of each
related entity shall be prepared on a fiscal year as may be selected by
the Grand Treasurer in consultation with the governing board of such
entity;

G.

Act as Treasurer of various Boards, Boards of Trustees and other
entities of Grand Lodge as provided for elsewhere in this Code, but
these duties do not require that he serve as a member of such Boards,
Boards of Trustees or other entity of Grand Lodge;

H.

Select, when appropriate, with the approval of the Grand Master, a
stock and bond brokerage firm or firms or dealer or agent to purchase
or sell bonds and other securities of Grand Lodge and its entities in the
manner and as authorized by the Investment Committee and the laws
and ordinances of this Grand Lodge; and to designate, when
appropriate, with the approval of the Grand Master, a nominee or a
custodian nominee for the securities;

I.

Be responsible for the management of the personnel of the Accounting
Section of the Grand Lodge office and to create and adopt job
descriptions for and define the duties of said personnel, however, they
are subject to the general administration of the Grand Secretary; and

J.

Pay over and transfer to his successor at the end of his term all funds
and property of Grand Lodge then in his possession.

Report of the Committee on Policy and General Purposes:
Currently, the fiscal year of Grand Lodge ends on October 15. The
Grand Master recommends that this be changed to October 31. Traditionally,
the fiscal year of the Grand Master was based upon a fiscal year ending with the
month in which the Annual Communication is held. This language was placed

GRAND LODGE F.& A.M. OF CALIFORNIA

181

in the Code when the Annual Communication was always held on the second
Monday of October. 2006 is the last year in which we can be assured that this
will be the case.
Starting next year, we will have changes in our dates and a different
starting point, with our Annual Communications being held on a Friday,
Saturday and Sunday, rather than Monday, Tuesday and Wednesday. This
flexibility in the scheduling of the Grand Lodge Sessions, permitting the Annual
Communication to be held within 20 days before or after the second Monday of
October, could create a substantial difficulty in the preparation of the financial
statements for each fiscal year of the Grand Lodge.
The Grand Master points out, "If the Annual Communication is in
September, the fiscal year could be one month short. If it is held in November it
could be one month longer."
This is not a good procedure for accounting practices, and so the Grand
Master has proposed that the Code be amended to provide that the fiscal year of
the Grand Lodge, for the purpose of preparing financial statements, would end,
every year, on October 31.
The change in the fiscal year might create a problem, were the Annual
Communication to be held within 20 days after the second Monday in October.
All bills would not necessarily be received and paid by the end of October,
particularly the bills submitted by the various officers and members of this
Grand Lodge with respect to their expense statements.
Nonetheless, the Grand Master's Recommendation will minimize the
confusion, and suitable arrangements can be made for the reimbursement of
expenses and the payment of all other Grand Lodge obligations that might be
incurred before, during or after the Grand Lodge Session.
This Recommendation is consistent with the policies and general
purposes of this Grand Lodge.
Recommendation ADOPTED.
Grand Master Sorsabal’s Recommendation No. 6
Legislative Calendar for Annual Communication
The California Masonic Code presently requires that consideration of
legislation be taken up on the second day of the Annual Communication. Due
to an oversight in the past we have actually taken up some of the legislation on
the first day in order to take care of all the business before us. Having noted
that the present law is not consistent with our needs, I propose that we change to
law to enable us to take up legislation on the first or the second day – or both –
as determined by the Grand Master. This change will be especially important

182

PROCEEDINGS OF THE

when we change our Annual Communication in 2007 and 2008 to a weekend. I
therefore ask you to support this change in our code to give us the flexibility we
need to start the consideration of legislation on the first day of the Annual
Communication if necessary.
If you adopt this recommendation, Part V of the Ordinances of the
Code would be amended to read as marked:

PART V
RULES OF PROCEDURE FOR ANNUAL
AND SPECIAL COMMUNICATIONS

The following Rules of Procedure shall govern the deliberations at
each Annual and Special Communication of the Grand Lodge.
All committee reports must be in the hands of the Grand Secretary by
the close of business on the first day opening of the Communication.
Legislation shall be heard at the sessions on the second and subsequent days of
the Communication. Each chairman of a committee to which legislation has
been referred should be present at all times during the sessions at which
legislation will be considered until all reports from his committee have been
given. If a committeeman is not present and prepared to report when called for,
the Grand Secretary shall read the committee’s report. If the Grand Secretary
does not then have a copy of the report to read, the vote will be taken without
that report.
The Jurisprudence Committee shall report only once, unless there is a
special matter before the Grand Lodge which the committee and presiding
officer believe warrant individual attention. The presiding officer shall
announce the vote required for passage of each item before discussion begins.
Any legislation to be considered which has not been mailed to the
Lodges shall be printed in advance and distributed at or before the session at
which the matter shall be considered. No legislation may be modified at any
time without the consent of the proponent or his authorized representative
written on the face of the modified proposal. If any proposal is modified after it
has been made available to the delegates, any changes must be clearly brought
to the attention of the delegates at the Grand Lodge session by the presiding
officer. If not so presented by the presiding officer, the proposed modification
shall NOT be a part of the adopted legislation.
The Grand Secretary shall not read the minutes of any session during
the Communication. The minutes will be printed instead in the proceedings of
the Annual Communication.

GRAND LODGE F.& A.M. OF CALIFORNIA

183

When discussion of each resolution or other item of business is called
for by the presiding officer, the proponent, or his authorized representative,
shall have the option of speaking first or last. The proponent shall be allotted
five minutes. All other speakers from the floor shall be allotted three minutes
each.
If the proponent speaks first, a speaker opposed to the matter shall
speak next, and the pro and con speakers shall alternate thereafter. If the
proponent chooses to speak last, a speaker in opposition shall begin the
discussion, and the speakers shall alternate thereafter. Only three speakers on
each side of an issue, including the proponent, shall be permitted on each item
under discussion. Speakers in favor shall speak from the microphone on the
Grand Master’s left. Speakers opposed shall speak from the microphone on the
Grand Master’s right.
There shall be no lights on the floor nor shall any speaker’s
microphone be cut off. There shall be signal lights at the Grand Master’s
station. The presiding officer shall advise the speaker when his time has expired
and request that he immediately conclude his remarks.
If circumstances or the issue under discussion warrant, the presiding
officer, in his sole discretion, may waive any of the guidelines set forth above.
For example, the presiding officer at his discretion may grant speakers
additional time or may give more than six speakers permission to present their
views. It is contemplated that the waiver of these rules would only occur on
issues of great importance to the Craft.
Report of the Committee on Policy and General Purposes:
Grand Master Sorsabal’s Recommendation 2006-06 seeks to make a
procedural modification to PART V of our Masonic Code, allowing the Grand
Master to begin the consideration of Legislation as part of the business of the
Grand Lodge on either the first or second day of the Annual Communication
instead of waiting for the second day, as the Code now provides.
This change will give the Grand Master the flexibility with this
important task which may be important to help him manage our Annual
Communications under the flexible calendar which was adopted by this Grand
Lodge and which becomes effective with our next Annual Communication.
This Recommendation is of a housekeeping nature which will bring
our rules and procedures for future Communications into harmony with recent
changes in those rules and procedures. This Recommendation is consistent with
our past policies seeking to establish consistency in our laws.

184

PROCEEDINGS OF THE

This Recommendation is consistent with the policies and general
purposes of this Grand Lodge.

Recommendation ADOPTED.

CARRY-OVER FROM 2005 ANNUAL COMMUNICATION
Grand Master David Doan’s Recommendation No. 2005-02
Use of Raffles by Masonic Organizations
It has long been the policy of the Grand Lodge of California to prohibit
gambling. It is not appropriate for our lodges to use the proceeds of gambling to
fund their activities. A lodge is expected to fund its activities from the funds it
obtains from its members and the name of Masonry can not be used to promote
gambling. This policy has helped our great fraternity earn the positive
reputation that we enjoy.
It has come to my attention that some of our Concordant and
Appendant Masonic Organizations have been violating the CMC by conducting
raffles. I have taken action to stop those violations. As a result, I have been
informed of violations by other groups including some of our youth groups.
Each of these violations were to benefit a charity or for a youth group to raise
funds for some special event. Whether it is called a raffle or an opportunity
drawing it is really a raffle in the meaning of the CMC. As I looked at the
problem it has become clear to me that there is a need for change. As our
membership declines, our monetary support for our charities and youth groups
has also diminished. In some cases our support for our youth groups has
declined very significantly. While our charities have other options for raising
funds to meet their needs, our youth groups do not have many options.
Society’s acceptance of lotteries for the benefit of education and
changes to state law make it clear that society will not have a negative view of a
limited use of raffles for the benefit of charitable causes or our youth groups for
special events such as paying the cost of attending leadership camps or the cost
of attending state and national meetings. As long as the money raised by the
raffle is not used for the normal operating expenses of the organization, they do
not give aid and countenance of Masonry to gambling enterprises and the raffles
are conducted in accordance with all federal, state and local laws they should be
authorized. Under no circumstances shall a Craft Lodge accept any of the
proceeds or profits of any raffle authorized by this change.
Resolution 05-12 seeks to change our laws to permit fund raising
through gambling. This resolution would also remove the authority of the
Grand Master to act against a Mason who promotes gambling enterprises
conducted in the name of, or for the benefit of, any lodge or Masonic
organization.
This resolution goes too far and would allow the name of
Masonry to be used to promote gambling and would allow lodges to benefit
from the proceeds of gambling. I can not support that resolution and have
proposed this recommendation to address the needs of our concordant and
appendant bodies while maintaining the integrity of our lodges. The change that

PROCEEDINGS OF THE

186

I am recommending is similar to the change you authorized a few years ago to
lift the restrictions on advertising from our concordant and appendant bodies.
This change will only apply to Concordant and Appendant Masonic
Organizations and not to the Craft Lodges.
It is therefore my recommendation that we authorize Concordant and
Appendant Masonic Organizations to conduct raffles for the benefit of their
charities and for our Masonic Youth Orders to be able to conduct raffles for
their charities and special needs such as leadership development and travel to
special events.
If you adopt this recommendation, the following changes will be made
to the Code. Section 1060 of the Code would be amended to read as marked:

§1060. DUTIES.
The Grand Master shall:
A.

Preside in Grand Lodge at all Annual and Special Communications.
There shall be no appeal from his rulings as presiding officer;

B.

Present, at each Annual Communication, a written message setting
forth:
1.

All his official acts during the year;

2.

The general condition of Masonry within the Jurisdiction; and

3.
Recommending such legislation as he deems necessary or
expedient for the welfare of the Craft;
C.

Constitute the Lodge for which a charter is granted in accordance with
the ancient usages and regulations. He may do so either in person or
by a duly authorized representative;

D.

Cause to be personally served on any Mason appearing to be engaged
in the conduct or promotion of any raffle, lottery or other form of
gambling not permitted hereunder that is being conducted, or is about
to be conducted, in the name of, or for the benefit of any committee or
other subordinate body of any Lodge or Masonic Organization, a
notice requiring that Mason to immediately discontinue his
participation.
If the Mason fails to discontinue his participation in such enterprise,
the Grand Master shall refer the matter to a Lodge of this Jurisdiction
for trial on the charge of unmasonic conduct. The Junior Warden of
the Lodge shall prefer the charges against the Mason. Pending the
trial, the Grand Master may suspend the Mason from all the rights and
benefits of Masonry;

GRAND LODGE F.& A.M. OF CALIFORNIA

187

E.

Exercise general and careful supervision over the Craft, and see that
the Constitution and Ordinances of Grand Lodge are strictly
maintained, supported, and obeyed; and

F.

Discharge all necessary executive functions of Grand Lodge when it is
not in session.

Section 15090 of the Code would be amended to read as marked:

§15090. GAMBLING.
Neither a Lodge, a Masonic Organization, nor a Mason shall, directly or
indirectly, give or attempt to give the aid or countenance of Masonry to, or
participate in, the conduct or preparation of any raffle, lottery or other form of
gambling, conducted in the name of, or directly or indirectly for the monetary
benefit of, any Lodge or of any Masonic Organization. A Lodge shall not
accept any of the proceeds or profits of any raffle, lottery or other gambling
enterprise.
Prizes may be awarded if not purchased from monies collected as admission
fees and if no separate charge is made for the chance to win.
The foregoing notwithstanding, Masonic Organizations, and their members may
conduct raffles for the benefit of their charities and Masonic Youth Orders may
conduct raffles for their charities and special needs such as leadership
development and travel to special events, if conducted strictly in accordance
with all applicable federal, state and local laws and with the provisions
hereunder related to financial support.

Report of the Committee on Policy and General Purposes:
Grand Master Doan’s Recommendation 20055-02 seeks to change
section 15090 of the California Masonic Code to permit Masonic Organizations
and their members, as well as Youth Organizations, to conduct raffles or
lotteries to raise money for their charities and specific needs, if conducted in
accordance with all applicable Federal, State and Local laws.
The temptation to seek an easy way to raise funds is forever present,
but the strict Masonic Laws regarding gambling which were framed by our
forefathers have served well to protect our reputation from scandal or from
negative community opinions of our Fraternity.
The laws controlling gambling in California are detailed and complex.
Only “eligible organizations” may legally conduct a raffle; Masonic

188

PROCEEDINGS OF THE

Organizations may not be eligible. Those organizations which do qualify must
provide detailed information and be subject to governmental audits.
Organizations are exempt from registration if they are religious
organizations, cemetery corporations or charitable corporations organized as
religious organizations, educational institutions, hospitals or health care plans.
A raffle is exempt from registration if it satisfies, among other things,
that the scheme does not require any participant to pay for a chance to win.
Finally, notwithstanding that these changes only apply to our
concordant, appendant and youth organizations, every person who lets or
permits any building to be used for the purposes of conducting an unauthorized
raffle is committing a crime.
It has forever been the policy of our Grand Lodge to enact such
legislation which would protect our reputation and good standing in our
communities. Venturing into the world of gambling and encouraging our youth
organizations to participate as well, may dim our luster and bring our morals
and ethics into question.
This recommendation is not consistent with the policies and general
purposes of this Grand Lodge.

Recommendation ADOPTED.
Grand Master David Doan’s Recommendation No. 2005-05
Optional Grand Lodge Officers
There has been a great deal of concern about the expenses of Grand
Lodge and continued pressure to raise the per capita each year to meet the
increased operating costs and to make up for the declining membership. Grand
Lodge has made significant cuts in its expense where it could without reducing
the quality of the services provided to the craft. Until our membership numbers
stabilize, projected to be in another 8 years, we will need to continue to look for
ways to reduce our costs.
One area that is not available to a Grand Master is the reduction in the
number of Grand Lodge Officers. CMC Sections 230 and 520 require all but
the Assistant Grand Organist and the Assistant Grand Tiler to appointed and
installed. I propose to give the Grand Master the option to appoint four other
Grand Lodge officers, the Grand Standard Bearer, Grand Sword Bearer, Grand
Bible Bearer, and Grand Pursuivant. While each of these officers has an
important role in our Grand Lodge Ceremonies, their presence is not crucial and
can be eliminated if the financial needs of the Grand Lodge as determined by
the Grand Master require them to be temporally left vacant. As the financial

GRAND LODGE F.& A.M. OF CALIFORNIA

189

condition of Grand Lodge changes the Grand Master can choose to appoint or
not appoint these officers.
This recommendation makes it the sole discretion of the Grand Master
to appoint or not appoint these officers.
If you adopt this recommendation, the following changes will be made
to the Code. Section 230 of the Code would be amended to read as marked:

§230. MEMBERS AND MANNER OF ADDRESS.
Grand Lodge shall be composed of a Grand Master (whose manner of address
shall be Most Worshipful), a Deputy Grand Master, a Senior Grand Warden,
and a Junior Grand Warden (whose manner of address shall be Right
Worshipful), a Grand Treasurer, a Grand Secretary and a Grand Lecturer
(whose manner of address shall be Very Worshipful), a Grand Chaplain (whose
manner of address shall be Very Reverend), the Assistant Grand Lecturers, a
Grand Orator, an Assistant Grand Secretary, a Grand Marshal, a Grand Standard
Bearer, a Grand Sword Bearer, a Grand Bible Bearer, a Senior Grand Deacon, a
Junior Grand Deacon, a Senior Grand Steward, a Junior Grand Steward, a
Grand Pursuivant, a Grand Organist, a Grand Tiler, and, at the discretion of the
Grand Master, a Grand Standard Bearer, a Grand Sword Bearer, a Grand Bible
Bearer, a Grand Pursuivant, an Assistant Grand Organist and an Assistant Grand
Tiler (whose manner of address shall be Worshipful), together with all Past
Grand Officers and Past Masters of this Jurisdiction, and the Master and
Wardens of the several chartered and duly constituted Lodges (except Research
Lodges) or their representatives duly elected. (10/97)
A Past Grand Lodge officer is one of the elective Grand Lodge officers who has
been regularly elected and installed and has served his term in Grand Lodge.
(10/96)

Report of the Committee on Policy and General Purposes:
Grand Master Doan’s Recommendation No. 2005-05 seeks to amend
Section 230 of the California Masonic Code to allow the Grand Master the sole
discretion of appointing or not appointing the Grand Standard Bearer, Grand
Sword Bearer, Grand Bible Bearer, and Grand Pursuivant. He feels this
recommendation is a cost-cutting device which will relieve some of the pressure
of raising per capita. He states that Grand Lodge has made significant cuts in its
operating expenses and if it makes more the quality of Grand Lodge services
will be reduced.
These Grand Offices have existed for many years in the long history of
the Grand Lodge of California. There would be great inconsistency if one Grand
Master chooses to appoint some of these Grand Officers one year and the next

190

PROCEEDINGS OF THE

Grand Master chooses to fill a different set of offices, or none at all, the
following year. The ritual of opening Grand Lodge would be affected every
year according the number of Grand Lodge Officers selected by the Grand
Master, thereby promoting confusion, and possibly discord.
This Recommendation is not consistent with the policies and general
purposes of this Grand Lodge.

Report of the Committee on Finance:
The Finance Committee has examined the financial impact of this
legislation and has found that it could result in a reduction of approximately
$15,000 or about $0.25 on the per capita. It should be noted that because of the
timing of this legislation the first year of impact if it were adopted would be the
2007/2008 Grand Lodge Year. In addition, since it is optional with the Grand
master any savings would depend upon the decision of the incoming Grand
Master not to appoint certain optional Grand Lodge Officers

Recommendation NOT ADOPTED.
Grand Master David Doan’s Recommendation No. 2005-07
Three Year Evaluation of Chance to Advance
Clearly during the last several years, there has been considerable
interest generated within the Craft in California concerning One-Day
Conferrals. It has appeared as a piece of legislation at our Annual
Communication the last two years and while a majority of those attending have
supported with the legislation, it did not obtain the necessary votes to pass.
In the past 5 years, the Grand Lodge of California has initiated 6,731
candidates. Of the candidates initiated between July 1, 1999 and June 30, 2004,
2,219 have failed to be raised to the Sublime Degree of Master Mason. That
means that 33% of those who became Entered Apprentice Masons in these five
years did not become Master Masons. We lost a third of our candidates.
The failure of these candidates to progress beyond Entered Apprentice
or Fellow Craft has resulted in a tremendous loss to the Fraternity in California.
These advancements may not be solely the fault of the candidates as some of
the investigating committees and coaches are often ill-prepared for the
responsibilities they are given. This can result in candidates who do not know
what is expected of them until they have already been initiated. Also, when
Masonic principles and symbolism are not adequately explained to the
candidates; the result has too often been candidates who elect to drop out rather
than continue a process that appears to be both discouraging and difficult to

GRAND LODGE F.& A.M. OF CALIFORNIA

191

them. The Lodge may have truly failed the candidate rather than the candidate
failing Masonry.
It is my belief that our California Masonic Code should be amended to
allow a three year pilot program to allow the Grand Master to permit lodges to
confer the Fellow Craft and Master Mason Degrees on those candidates who
have not progressed in a constituent California lodge for at least one year. Such
conferral could be done in one day, or more than one day – but the presently
required proficiency between the degrees would be waived for this purpose. If
this recommendation were adopted, the California Masonic Code would be
amended to add Section 26285 as follows:

§26285. CONFERRAL OF DEGREES
Notwithstanding Section 26280 of the California Masonic Code, the Grand
Master shall have the authority to provide for the conferral of the Fellow Craft
and Master Mason degrees on candidates in one day, without satisfying the
proficiency and Masonic education requirements of the Code, provided:
A.

Candidates have previously received the Entered Apprentice Degree or
Fellow Craft Degree in a constituent Lodge at a date more than one
year previous to the conferral allowed by this section;

B.

Candidates must request such conferral, and be approved by his Lodge;

C.

Candidates shall be required to attend a Masonic Education program on
the day of the conferral for each degree. All candidates shall attend the
Masonic Education element for the Entered Apprentice and Fellow
Craft Degrees. This Masonic Education program for each degree shall
be provided by the Grand Lodge and shall consist of an explanation of
the history, philosophy and symbolism of each degree together with an
explanation of the meanings of the obligations;

D.

An examination to determine the candidates’ knowledge shall be
administered by the Grand Lodge at the conclusion of the Masonic
Education for each degree;

E.

Each Lodge participating in the conferral of the degrees shall provide
one knowledgeable mentor at the conferral site for each candidate from
that Lodge to assist in explaining the process and purposes of the
degrees; and

F.

This section shall be automatically repealed three years from the date
of its adoption, except as follows. At the end of this three year
program, the program of conferral provided herein shall be evaluated.
The Grand Master shall cause a report on the results of the pilot
program to be completed and presented to Grand Lodge. If the

192

PROCEEDINGS OF THE

program is to be continued beyond this three year period, it shall
require a resolution or Grand Master’s recommendation presented to
Grand Lodge and approved in the manner required for amendments to
these Ordinances.

Report of the Committee on Policy and General Purposes:
This Recommendation points to an issue that is the life blood of
Freemasonry. That is to initiate, pass and raise a good man to the Sublime
Degree of Master Mason. The numbers indicate, over a five year period, that
thirty-three percent of all candidates did not proceed past Entered Apprentice or
Fellowcraft to become Master Masons. There are many reasons why this has
occurred; there is no single solution.
This Recommendation provides for a One-Day class, but in a different
format from that proposed two years ago, and ultimately defeated last year.
There are those that would argue the merits of such a change, but if change is
inevitable, the path of least resistance can be helpful. This Recommendation
provides the path of least resistance. The proposed new California Masonic
Code section, permitting those who have not advanced after one year to take
their remaining degrees in one day, will be not mandatory; a lodge would have
to give its consent. Thus, this recommendation, if enacted, would not
necessarily have impact on the customs used by any particular lodge. In other
words, if a lodge does not want to participate, it does not have to.
This Recommendation has been proposed in such a way that it will not
have a long term effect. It is proposed as a pilot program with a report on the
results due at the end of the third year. If the program is successful, it can be
made permanent, but only if it is proposed again, either in a Grand Master’s
Recommendation, or in a proposed Resolution. This legislation is a viable tool
for Grand Lodge and its members.
This Recommendation is consistent with the policies and general
purposes of this Grand Lodge.

Recommendation NOT ADOPTED.
